```text
   +-------------------+----------------------------+--------------------+
   | AXIS              | BERKELEY - SUBJECTIVE      | HEGEL - ABSOLUTE   |
   +-------------------+----------------------------+--------------------+
   | Basic reality     | finite spirits, God, ideas | self-developing    |
   |                   |                            | Absolute Spirit    |
   | Why objects are   | sensible existence CONSISTS| objectivity is a   |
   | mind-dependent    | in being perceived         | MOMENT within      |
   |                   |                            | dialectical self-  |
   |                   |                            | development        |
   | Role of the       | an infinite PERCEIVER who  | not an external    |
   | divine/system     | causes and coordinates     | perceiver          |
   |                   | ideas                      | preserving private |
   |                   |                            | idea-collections   |
   | Method            | anti-abstraction plus      | dialectical        |
   |                   | immaterialist argument     | development through|
   |                   |                            | determinate        |
   |                   |                            | negation           |
   | VERDICT           | both reject self-subsisting matter as final     |
   |                   | reality, but theistic SUBJECTIVE idealism is    |
   |                   | NOT an early version of OBJECTIVE/ABSOLUTE      |
   |                   | idealism - the difference is in KIND            |
   +-------------------+----------------------------+--------------------+
```

⚠️ **Caption.** Both comparisons are examiner-set, and both are lost by scripts that write two separate mini-essays. Use matched axes: attack, positive result, method, verdict.

#### 1. Spirit — the one substance that survives

- ✅ The perceiving mind is a **real active substance**: it is the perceiver, not a bundle of perceptions. Berkeley **keeps the self**, which is exactly where Hume will refuse to follow him.
- ✅ We have no **idea** of spirit, because ideas are passive and the mind is active; we have a **notion** of it — a direct awareness that does not fit the impression-to-idea schema.
- ⚠️ **The asymmetry and its defence:** critics call the notion doctrine ad hoc. Berkeley's reply is that it is *forced*: an idea, being passive, could never picture an activity, so if activity is known at all it must be known otherwise. The reply is principled; whether it is sufficient is ❓ genuinely disputed.

#### 2. God — derived, not assumed

1. Ideas are passive and inert; they cannot cause anything.
2. Yet the ideas of sense come to me involuntarily and in a steady, regular order.
3. Whatever produces them must be an active spirit, and one whose power and wisdom exceed mine.
4. Therefore God, and the "laws of nature" are the settled grammar of God's **language** of ideas — regularities are **signs**, not causes.
5. Therefore objects persist unperceived by finite minds because they are always perceived by the infinite Mind.

- ✅ "Does the tree exist when no one perceives it?" — **Yes**, because God always perceives it.
- ✅ Berkeley thus uses empiricist premises to **defend theism**: the material world is replaced by God's continual ideation, and his idealism is **theocentric**, not sceptical.
- ⚠️ **The strongest objection:** this makes "existence unperceived" into a hypothesis about an unobserved being — the very move Berkeley forbade Locke. His answer is the notion doctrine of §1.

#### 3. Common sense, and the honest limit of the defence

- ✅ Berkeley claims to be closer to common sense than the representative realist, because the plain man believes he perceives things directly and never believed in a veil.
- ⚠️ The claim is strong about **objects** and weaker about **causes**: the plain man also believes that fire heats water, whereas for Berkeley fire is a sign and God is the agent. Concede this and the answer reads as an assessment rather than a defence brief.
- ⚠️ **The Johnson anecdote** — kicking a stone with "I refute it thus!" — is **reception, not refutation**. Berkeley never denied that the stone is felt; the kick confirms an idea of resistance, which is precisely what he says a stone is.

#### 4. Moore and Russell — the 2018 comparison, decided

- ✅ **Moore** attacks the idealist inference from "experienced" to "dependent on experience" by distinguishing an **act of awareness** from its **object**: the awareness of blue is not identical with blue. His transparency argument supports **direct realism** — the object is presented *through*, but not constituted by, consciousness.
- ✅ **Russell** also rejects Berkeleyan idealism, but develops an **analytical reconstruction**: ordinary physical objects are inferred or logically constructed from sense-data, perspectives and relations rather than accepted through Moorean direct realism.
- ⚠️ **Common ground:** both resist reducing object to act or idea. **The difference:** Moore stresses the act–object distinction and common-sense directness; Russell stresses logical construction and structural objectivity — so Moore *defends* the ordinary object while Russell *replaces* it.

#### 5. Berkeley and Hegel — the 2024 comparison, decided

- ✅ For Berkeley, reality is ideas in **finite and divine minds**; for Hegel, reality is the self-development of **Absolute Spirit** through dialectic.
- ✅ Berkeley's idealism is **subjective** — mind-dependence of sensible existence; Hegel's is **objective or absolute** — Mind developing itself as the world's substance.
- ⚠️ **The verdict sentence:** they agree only in refusing independently self-subsisting matter as final reality. Berkeley's God is an infinite **perceiver** who coordinates idea-collections; Hegel's Absolute is not an external perceiver at all. Calling Berkeley "an early Hegel" is a category error, and 2024 Q2(a) is testing exactly that discrimination.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Berkeley's God is a structural requirement of immaterialism rather than a devotional addition, and this is what fixes both the strength and the vulnerability of his idealism.
> **Named evidence.** *Principles* §§6, 30–33 and 48 argue that ideas are passive, that the ideas of sense arrive involuntarily in a settled order, and that only an active spirit of greater power and wisdom can produce them, while §§27, 89 and 140 introduce the doctrine that spirits are known by notion rather than by idea.
> **Analysis.** Since the infinite spirit both causes sense-ideas and secures their continuity when finite minds look away, God performs precisely the two functions Locke had assigned to matter, so immaterialism should be assessed as a substitution of explanatory posits rather than as their elimination.
> **Qualification.** The substitution is not obviously an advance, because an unperceivable spirit known only by notion is exposed to the same complaint Berkeley levelled at an unperceivable substratum — although his reply that a passive idea could never picture an activity gives the asymmetry a principled and not merely convenient basis.

#### Exam link

- **2018 Q3(a), 20 marks** — §§1–2 establish "Mind and its ideas alone are real"; §4 supplies both reactions **and** the difference between them, which is the third limb of that question.
- **2024 Q2(a), 20 marks** — §5 is the second limb, and it must be answered on matched axes.
- **2021 Q1(c), 10 marks** — §3 supplies the common-sense framing that makes "immediately perceived" more than a slogan.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| Treating God as an afterthought | God is derived from the passivity of ideas; present the derivation |
| Saying Berkeley has an *idea* of spirit | He has a **notion**; ideas are passive and cannot picture activity |
| Using Johnson's stone as a refutation | It is reception; the felt resistance is exactly what Berkeley calls the stone |
| Writing Moore and Russell as one reaction | 2018 explicitly asks for the **difference**: direct realism versus logical construction |
| Calling Berkeley an early Hegel | Subjective versus absolute idealism differ in kind, not degree |

#### Revision notes

- Ontology: spirits (active) + ideas (passive); no third category.
- Spirit known by **notion** (§§27, 89, 140), not by idea.
- God derived: inert ideas + involuntary order → an active infinite spirit; continuity follows.
- Laws of nature = the settled grammar of a divine language; signs, not causes.
- Other minds by analogy; solipsism blocked but knowledge of spirits is less immediate.
- Moore: act–object distinction, direct realism. Russell: logical construction, structural objectivity.
- Berkeley subjective and theistic; Hegel absolute and dialectical — different in kind.

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — BERKELEY III: SPIRITS, GOD AND THE COMPARISONS

```text
IDEAS ARE PASSIVE -> THEY CANNOT CAUSE ANYTHING
        |
        v
SENSE-IDEAS ARRIVE INVOLUNTARILY AND IN SETTLED ORDER
        |
        v
ONLY SPIRITS ACT -> AN INFINITE SPIRIT PRODUCES THEM = GOD
        |
        +--> CONTINUITY: the unperceived persists because God perceives it
        +--> LAWS OF NATURE = the grammar of a divine language; SIGNS not causes
        +--> SPIRIT KNOWN BY NOTION (Sec.27, 89, 140), not by idea
        +--> OTHER MINDS by ANALOGY from purposive signs
        |
        v
OBJECTION: an unperceivable spirit repeats the fault of an unknowable substratum
REPLY: a passive idea could never picture activity - the asymmetry is forced
        |
        v
2018 COMPARISON: MOORE act-object distinction -> DIRECT REALISM
                 RUSSELL sense-data + relations -> LOGICAL CONSTRUCTION
                 difference: Moore DEFENDS the object, Russell REPLACES it
2024 COMPARISON: BERKELEY subjective + theistic vs HEGEL absolute + dialectical
                 different in KIND, not degree
TRAP: God as afterthought | "idea of spirit" | Johnson's stone as refutation
ANSWER LINE: God does matter's old job, which is the real question about the system
```

### SESSION 10 — HUME I: IMPRESSIONS AND IDEAS, THE COPY PRINCIPLE, ASSOCIATION, THE FORK, AND THE DISSOLUTION OF SUBSTANCE

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** Hume divides everything in the mind into vivid originals and faint copies, insists that every simple copy must have had an original, sorts all enquiry into two boxes — what can be worked out by thinking alone and what must be found out by experience — and then asks which box "substance" belongs in. It fits neither.

**Technical definition:** Hume's science of human nature divides all **perceptions** into forceful **impressions** and their faint **ideas**, states the **copy principle** that every simple idea derives from a corresponding simple impression, explains the mind's connections by three principles of **association** — resemblance, contiguity and cause and effect — and demarcates enquiry by the **fork** between **relations of ideas** and **matters of fact**, with the consequence that "substance", lacking any source impression, is a **fiction of the imagination**.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Hume's forensic method has a single form that he applies to substance, to the self and to causal power alike: name the alleged idea, demand the impression from which it is copied, and if none can be produced, reclassify the idea as a fiction produced by the imagination — so the destructive results are not separate discoveries but one instrument used three times, and the instrument itself, the copy principle, is an empirical generalisation Hume admits admits an exception.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **perceptions → impressions and ideas** — the exhaustive division
- **force and vivacity** — the criterion distinguishing them
- **copy principle** — every simple idea copies a simple impression
- **missing shade of blue** — the exception Hume concedes
- **association: resemblance, contiguity, cause and effect**
- **relations of ideas** versus **matters of fact** — the fork
- **"commit it then to the flames"** — *Enquiry* XII, aimed at divinity or school metaphysics
- **fiction of the imagination** — the verdict on substance

**How to use them:** state the division and the copy principle, then apply the test to substance in three lines, then set out the fork as a table, and finally concede the missing shade **before** the examiner can use it against you.

#### Plain-language visual — the test, run once

```text
   THE HUMEAN AUDIT                     applied here to "SUBSTANCE"
   +---------------------------------------------------------------+
   | Q1  What idea are you claiming to have?                        |
   |     -> "an underlying something that HAS the qualities"        |
   +---------------------------------------------------------------+
   | Q2  From which IMPRESSION was that idea copied?                |
   |     -> Look. You find yellow. Heavy. Malleable. Shiny.         |
   |        You never find the HAVER, only the HAD.                 |
   +---------------------------------------------------------------+
   | Q3  No impression? Then what produced the idea?                |
   |     -> The imagination. Qualities that constantly go together  |
   |        are grouped, and a "support" is INVENTED for them.      |
   +---------------------------------------------------------------+
   | VERDICT: "substance" is a FICTION OF THE IMAGINATION -         |
   |          and this applies to MATERIAL and SPIRITUAL substance  |
   |          alike. Berkeley kept spirit; Hume does not.           |
   +---------------------------------------------------------------+
```

**In simple words:** Hume does not argue that there is no substance. He argues that you cannot say what you mean by it, because you cannot produce the experience the word was supposed to name. That is a claim about **content**, and keeping it a claim about content rather than about existence is what protects the answer from the standard misreading.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Impressions | vivid, forceful originals: sensations, passions, emotions |
| Ideas | faint copies used in thinking and remembering |
| Copy principle | every **simple** idea derives from a simple impression |
| Association | resemblance · contiguity in time or place · cause and effect |
| Relations of ideas | a priori, necessary; denial is self-contradictory |
| Matters of fact | a posteriori, contingent; the contrary is always conceivable |

> **One-line memory:** no impression, no legitimate idea — and no third box beyond the fork.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the architecture of the mind, the fork and the first casualty

```text
   ALL CONTENTS OF THE MIND = PERCEPTIONS
   =====================================================================
        IMPRESSIONS  (force and vivacity)        IDEAS (faint copies)
        +---------------------------+            +--------------------+
        | of SENSATION: colours,    |  copied    | memory: retains    |
        | sounds, tastes, pains     |  ------>   | order and vivacity |
        | of REFLECTION: passions,  |            | imagination: free  |
        | emotions, the felt        |            | to separate and    |
        | determination of the mind |            | recombine          |
        +---------------------------+            +--------------------+
   COPY PRINCIPLE: every SIMPLE idea derives from a corresponding SIMPLE
   impression. COMPLEX ideas may be new combinations (a golden mountain).
   CONCEDED EXCEPTION - THE MISSING SHADE OF BLUE (Treatise I.i.1; Enquiry II):
   a man acquainted with every shade of blue but one, shown the spectrum with
   a gap, can form the idea of the missing shade from imagination alone.
   Hume calls the instance "so singular" that he will not alter the maxim.
   WHY IT MATTERS FOR MARKS: it shows the copy principle is an EMPIRICAL
   GENERALISATION, not an analytic truth - so the demolitions of substance,
   self and necessary connexion rest on a premise Hume admits has an exception.
   HUME'S AVAILABLE REPLY: the shade is generated by INTERPOLATION BETWEEN
   impressions actually had, so the principle's SPIRIT survives its LETTER.
   =====================================================================
   THE THREE PRINCIPLES OF ASSOCIATION - the mind's gentle force
        RESEMBLANCE  |  CONTIGUITY in time/place  |  CAUSE AND EFFECT
   These are not logical relations; they are the psychological mechanism
   that later explains BOTH the fiction of the self AND causal belief.
   =====================================================================
   HUME'S FORK
   +-------------------------------+-------------------------------+
   | RELATIONS OF IDEAS            | MATTERS OF FACT               |
   | known a priori, by thought    | known a posteriori, require   |
   | alone                         | experience                    |
   | necessary and certain;        | contingent; the contrary is   |
   | denial is self-contradictory  | always conceivable            |
   | "2+2=4"; "a bachelor is       | "the sun will rise tomorrow"; |
   | unmarried"                    | "fire causes heat"            |
   | do not depend on what exists  | depend on how the world       |
   | in the world                  | actually is                   |
   +-------------------------------+-------------------------------+
   CONSEQUENCE: anything that is NEITHER is meaningless - "commit it then
   to the flames" (Enquiry XII), aimed at divinity or school metaphysics.
   ANCESTRY: this is the direct ancestor of the logical positivists'
   verification principle - a link worth one clause, not a paragraph.
   =====================================================================
   FIRST CASUALTY: SUBSTANCE
   no impression of a substrate -> "substance" is a FICTION OF THE
   IMAGINATION -> both MATERIAL and SPIRITUAL substance dissolve ->
   an object is a stable BUNDLE of co-occurring qualities
```

⚠️ **Caption.** The fork must be drawn as a table, because the 2018 question is decided by placing two sentences correctly inside it. Do not let it become prose.

#### 1. The division, and the criterion

- ✅ All contents of the mind are **perceptions**, dividing into **impressions** — vivid, forceful, original experiences (sensations, passions) — and **ideas**, their faint copies used in memory and imagination.
- ⚠️ The criterion is **force and vivacity**, a difference of degree. Hume accepts that in fever or madness the difference can blur; the classification is a working psychology, not a definition by essence.

#### 2. The copy principle, stated with its exception

- ✅ Every simple **idea** traces back to a corresponding simple **impression**. If no source impression can be located, the alleged idea is suspect, obscure or fictitious.
- ⚠️ Note the precision that protects marks: for Hume the failure makes the idea **fictitious or obscure**, not automatically "meaningless" in the later logical-positivist sense. The stronger reading belongs to Topic 07.
- ❓ **The missing shade of blue** (*Treatise* I.i.1; *Enquiry* II) is Hume's own conceded counter-example, and he calls it "so singular" that it is not worth altering his general maxim for it.
- ⚠️ **Why it earns marks:** deploying it converts a descriptive answer into a critical one, because it shows the copy principle to be an empirical generalisation rather than an analytic truth. **Hume's available reply:** the missing shade is generated by interpolation *between* impressions actually had, so the principle's spirit survives even if its letter does not.

#### 3. Association — the engine of everything that follows

- ✅ Ideas are ordinarily connected through **resemblance**, **contiguity in time or place**, and **cause and effect**.
- ⚠️ Association is a **psychological** mechanism, not a logical relation, and it is decisive later: resemblance and causation together produce the fiction of a unified self, and repeated conjunction produces the felt determination that Hume identifies as the source of the idea of necessary connexion. Naming association early makes the later sessions look inevitable rather than surprising.

#### 4. The fork, and the 2018 question it decides

| Relations of ideas | Matters of fact |
|---|---|
| Known a priori; discoverable by thought alone | Known a posteriori; require experience |
| Necessary, certain; denial is self-contradictory | Contingent; the contrary is always conceivable |
| Examples: "2+2=4"; "a bachelor is unmarried" | Examples: "the sun will rise tomorrow"; "fire causes heat" |
| Do not depend on what exists in the world | Depend on how the world actually is |

- ✅ **Consequence:** any proposition that is neither a relation of ideas nor a matter of fact is meaningless — "commit it then to the flames" (*Enquiry* XII).
- ⚠️ **The 2018 answer in one line:** "2+2=4" is a relation of ideas whose denial is contradictory; "the sun will rise tomorrow" is a matter of fact whose denial remains perfectly conceivable. They are **not** of the same necessity, and saying they are equally probable is the error the question is designed to catch.
- ❓ **The reflexive objection:** is the fork itself a relation of ideas or a matter of fact? If neither, it refutes itself. Hume's available answer is that it is a clarification of the meanings of terms — a meta-level classification rather than a first-order claim.

#### 5. Substance dissolved

- ✅ Apply the copy principle: do we have an impression of substance? **No.** We have only impressions of particular qualities — redness, hardness, sweetness — never of an underlying substrate.
- ✅ Therefore "substance" is a **fiction of the imagination**: the mind's habit of grouping constantly conjoined qualities and *inventing* a support for them.
- ✅ Hume dissolves **both** material and spiritual substance; neither has a corresponding impression. **This is exactly where he passes Berkeley**, who retained spirit.
- ✅ **Qualities without substance:** there are only bundles of perceptions — qualities that co-occur regularly. The "object" is a stable bundle; the "self" is a flowing bundle.

#### 6. The external world — constancy, coherence and the double-existence theory

- ✅ Sense presents only **interrupted** perceptions; it does not present an object existing continuously while unperceived, or existing distinctly beyond perception.
- ✅ **Constancy** of similar perceptions and **coherence** in their reappearance lead the imagination to feign continued objects and to smooth perceptual gaps.
- ✅ Philosophers respond with a **double-existence theory** — private perceptions representing independently existing objects — but this cannot be verified, because all comparison remains within perceptions.
- ⚠️ **The crucial qualification:** Hume does **not** demonstrate that no external world exists. Sceptical reflection undermines the rational justification, while nature restores the unavoidable belief in bodies during ordinary life.
- ✅ This is the pattern of Humean naturalism, and it recurs in every later session: explain why a belief is psychologically inevitable **after** showing that reason cannot establish its metaphysical object.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Hume's dissolution of substance is the first application of a single instrument rather than an independent metaphysical thesis.
> **Named evidence.** The *Treatise* divides perceptions into impressions and ideas by force and vivacity and states that every simple idea copies a simple impression; introspection yields impressions of redness, hardness and sweetness but never of a bearer; and the *Enquiry* classifies all enquiry as relations of ideas or matters of fact, consigning what fits neither to the flames.
> **Analysis.** Because the demand for a source impression is a demand about **content** rather than about existence, the conclusion is that "substance" names no clear idea and is supplied instead by the imagination's habit of grouping constantly conjoined qualities — a result that applies with equal force to Berkeley's spirit, which is why Hume's empiricism is more consistent than his predecessor's.
> **Qualification.** The instrument is itself an empirical generalisation to which Hume concedes the missing shade of blue as an exception, so the demolition of substance is only as secure as a maxim its author admits is not exceptionless — although interpolation between impressions actually had preserves the principle's spirit if not its letter.

#### Exam link

- **2018 Q1(a), 10 marks** — "Are the two truths ... of the same necessity for Hume?" is decided entirely by §4.
- **2020 Q2(a), 20 marks** and **2021 Q1(e), 10 marks** — §§1–3 are the machinery those answers presuppose.
- Any "substance" question across the item uses §5, and any "external world" material uses §6.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "Hume denies that anything exists" | He denies the **content** of the idea of substance, not the existence of perceptions |
| Treating impressions and ideas as a difference in kind | The difference is **force and vivacity**, a difference of degree |
| Ignoring the missing shade | The examiner may raise it; concede it first and supply the interpolation reply |
| Collapsing relations of ideas into matters of fact | This is the exact error 2018 Q1(a) is built to catch |
| Saying Hume proves there is no external world | He shows reason cannot justify the belief; nature imposes it anyway |

#### Revision notes

- Perceptions = impressions (vivid) + ideas (faint copies); criterion is force and vivacity.
- Copy principle: every simple idea copies a simple impression; missing shade of blue is the conceded exception.
- Association: resemblance · contiguity · cause and effect — psychological, not logical.
- Fork: relations of ideas (a priori, necessary) versus matters of fact (a posteriori, contingent).
- "Commit it then to the flames" — *Enquiry* XII, aimed at divinity or school metaphysics.
- Substance: no impression → fiction of the imagination; material **and** spiritual alike.
- External world: constancy + coherence → feigned continuity; double existence unverifiable; nature restores belief.

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — HUME I: THE INSTRUMENT AND ITS FIRST CASUALTY

```text
ALL PERCEPTIONS = IMPRESSIONS (vivid) + IDEAS (faint copies)
        |
        v
COPY PRINCIPLE: every SIMPLE idea copies a SIMPLE impression
   CONCEDED EXCEPTION: the missing shade of blue (Treatise I.i.1; Enquiry II)
   REPLY: interpolation between impressions actually had - spirit survives
        |
        v
ASSOCIATION: RESEMBLANCE | CONTIGUITY | CAUSE AND EFFECT (psychological)
        |
        v
THE FORK
   RELATIONS OF IDEAS: a priori, necessary, denial contradictory  ("2+2=4")
   MATTERS OF FACT: a posteriori, contingent, contrary conceivable
                    ("the sun will rise tomorrow")
   NEITHER? -> "commit it then to the flames" (Enquiry XII)
        |
        v
APPLY TO SUBSTANCE: no impression of a bearer -> FICTION OF THE IMAGINATION
   dissolves MATERIAL and SPIRITUAL substance alike (Berkeley kept spirit)
        |
        v
EXTERNAL WORLD: interrupted perceptions -> CONSTANCY + COHERENCE ->
   imagination feigns continued bodies; double existence unverifiable;
   reason cannot justify, NATURE compels
TRAP: "nothing exists" | fork collapse | ignoring the missing shade
ANSWER LINE: one instrument, three casualties - substance, self, necessity
```

### SESSION 11 — HUME II: THE BUNDLE SELF, THE APPENDIX RETRACTION, KANT'S REPLY, AND THE FAILURE OF THE PROOFS OF GOD

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** When Hume looks inside for the self he finds only a rapid succession of particular experiences and never the experiencer; so the self, like substance, becomes a collection rather than a thing — and he later admits, with unusual honesty, that he cannot say what holds the collection together.

**Technical definition:** Hume's **bundle theory** holds that the self is "nothing but a bundle or collection of different perceptions, which succeed each other with an inconceivable rapidity, and are in a perpetual flux and movement" (*Treatise* I.iv.6); the apparent unity is a fiction produced by **resemblance** and **causation** among perceptions, and the Appendix to the *Treatise* records Hume's own failure to explain the principle that unites them.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Hume's statement that he can never catch himself without a perception is not a report of introspective bad luck but the copy principle applied to the perceiver: if every legitimate idea must be traced to an impression, and introspection delivers only particular perceptions in flux, then the simple continuing self is a fiction of the imagination — and the fact that Hume's own Appendix confesses he cannot say what binds the bundle is the strongest evidence that the fault lies in the atomism of impressions rather than in the self.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **"I never can catch myself"** — the introspective report the 2020 paper quotes
- **bundle or collection of different perceptions** — the exact formula
- **perpetual flux** — the phrase that rules out a simple identity
- **fiction of identity** — produced by resemblance and causation
- **the Appendix retraction** — "all my hopes vanish"
- **transcendental unity of apperception** — Kant's formal condition, not an entity
- **design argument, cosmological argument, miracles** — the three fronts on God

**How to use them:** answer the self question with doctrine, then the Appendix, then Kant — in that order, because 2020 Q2(a) requires the empiricist argument to be *complete* before the reply is reported.

#### Plain-language visual — looking for the looker

```text
   CLOSE YOUR EYES AND LOOK FOR YOURSELF.
   +-----------------------------------------------------------------+
   | You find: a twinge in the knee | the sound of a fan | the memory |
   | of breakfast | irritation | a patch of red behind the eyelids    |
   +-----------------------------------------------------------------+
   | You do NOT find: the one who is finding them.                    |
   +-----------------------------------------------------------------+
                                |
   SO WHY DOES IT FEEL LIKE ONE CONTINUOUS "I"?
   +-----------------------------------------------------------------+
   | RESEMBLANCE: today's ideas resemble yesterday's memories, and    |
   |              the imagination slides easily between them.         |
   | CAUSATION:   each perception seems to produce the next; memory   |
   |              links them into a chain.                            |
   | -> The EASY TRANSITION is mistaken for an IDENTICAL OBJECT.      |
   +-----------------------------------------------------------------+
                                |
   AND THEN, IN THE APPENDIX, HUME ADMITS DEFEAT:
   "all my hopes vanish when I come to explain the principles that
    unite our successive perceptions."
```

**In simple words:** Hume's argument has two halves and most scripts write only the first. The first half says there is no impression of a self. The second half explains why we nevertheless believe in one. The Appendix then adds a third element that no examiner expects: Hume says the explanation does not work. Include all three and the answer is complete.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| The report | "I never can catch myself at any time without a perception" |
| The thesis | the self is a bundle in perpetual flux |
| The explanation | resemblance and causation produce a felt continuity |
| The confession | the Appendix cannot state the uniting principle |
| What is denied | a **simple, identical self-substance**, not the perceptions |
| Kant's reply | a formal condition of unified experience, not an observed ego |

> **One-line memory:** Hume finds the **perceptions** and loses the **owner** — and says so himself.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the bundle, the fiction, the confession and the reply

```text
   THE ARGUMENT                                        Treatise I.iv.6
   =====================================================================
   1 COPY PRINCIPLE applied to the self: which impression is the self
     copied from? It would have to be constant and invariable through
     the whole course of a life.
   2 INTROSPECTION delivers only particular perceptions - heat, cold,
     light, shade, love, hatred, pain, pleasure - in perpetual flux.
   3 NO constant impression is found.
   4 THEREFORE the self is "nothing but a bundle or collection of
     different perceptions, which succeed each other with an inconceivable
     rapidity, and are in a perpetual flux and movement."
   =====================================================================
   BUT WHY THE ILLUSION OF UNITY?
        RESEMBLANCE  ---+
                        +--> the imagination passes SMOOTHLY along the
        CAUSATION    ---+    series and MISTAKES an easy transition for
                             an identical object -> the FICTION of self
   =====================================================================
   THE APPENDIX RETRACTION - the page that earns marks
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   | Hume cannot explain what BINDS the bundle: "all my hopes vanish    |
   | when I come to explain the principles that unite our successive    |
   | perceptions."                                                      |
   | DIAGNOSIS: he has denied BOTH a substance AND any real connexion   |
   | between distinct existences, so he has removed in advance every    |
   | resource a principle of unity could have used.                     |
   | THEREFORE the honest verdict: the failure indicts the ATOMISM OF   |
   | IMPRESSIONS, not the self.                                         |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   =====================================================================
   KANT'S REPLY - the 2020 second limb, stated exactly
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   | 1 Kant ACCEPTS that no impression or inner intuition reveals a     |
   |   simple soul-substance. He is not restoring Descartes' ego.       |
   | 2 He argues instead that the "I THINK" must be able to accompany   |
   |   ALL my representations if they are to belong to ONE experience.  |
   | 3 The TRANSCENDENTAL UNITY OF APPERCEPTION is therefore a FORMAL   |
   |   CONDITION of synthesising a manifold under rules - not another   |
   |   perceived item inside the bundle.                                |
   | 4 LIMIT: this answers the missing unity at the level of POSSIBLE   |
   |   EXPERIENCE; it does not prove an immortal soul or settle every   |
   |   practical question of identity through time.                     |
   | 5 HUMEAN REJOINDER: Kant has stated a condition of DESCRIPTION     |
   |   rather than discovered an ENTITY. Kant accepts the non-entity    |
   |   point and insists that rule-governed unity is PRESUPPOSED by     |
   |   judgment.                                                        |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   CROSS-TRADITION BRIDGE (one clause, not a paragraph): the bundle
   strikingly resembles the Buddhist no-self doctrine (nairatmyavada).
```

⚠️ **Caption.** Step 1 of Kant's reply is the step that separates a good answer from a wrong one. A script that reports Kant as "proving the soul exists" has inverted his position and will lose the second limb of the 2020 question entirely.

#### 1. The argument, and the exact formula

- ✅ Apply the copy principle to the self: **introspect, and you never find an impression of a "self"** — only particular perceptions in constant flux.
- ✅ The self is "**nothing but a bundle or collection of different perceptions, which succeed each other with an inconceivable rapidity, and are in a perpetual flux and movement**" (*Treatise* I.iv.6).
- ⚠️ **What is denied:** a *simple, identical self-substance*. What is **not** denied: the perceptions, which are real. The trap is to write "Hume says the self does not exist" — he says there is no **owner** behind the perceptions.

#### 2. Why we believe in a self anyway

- ✅ The imagination produces the fiction through **resemblance** — present ideas resemble past memories, creating a felt continuity — and through **causation**, since one perception seems to produce the next and memory links them.
- ⚠️ The mechanism is the same association introduced in Session 10; the identity we ascribe is therefore a **fictitious** identity, imposed on a succession because the transition between its members is smooth.

#### 3. The Appendix — Hume's own retraction

- ✅ In the Appendix to the *Treatise*, Hume confesses that he cannot satisfactorily explain what **binds** the bundle together: "all my hopes vanish when I come to explain the principles that unite our successive perceptions."
- ⚠️ **The diagnosis worth stating:** having denied both a substance and any real connexion between distinct existences, Hume has removed every resource a principle of unity might have used, so the failure is a symptom of the **atomism of impressions**, not of the self.
- ✅ This honest confession is philosophically significant and is the single most quotable page in any answer on Hume's self.
- ⚠️ Note also that the *Enquiry* silently **drops** the personal-identity discussion — a textual fact worth one clause, because it shows Hume's own dissatisfaction.

#### 4. Kant's response, stated without distortion

- ✅ Kant **accepts** that no impression or inner intuition reveals a simple soul-substance.
- ✅ He argues instead that the "**I think**" must be able to accompany all representations if they are to belong to one experience at all.
- ✅ The **transcendental unity of apperception** is a **formal condition** of synthesising a manifold under rules, not another perceived item inside the bundle.
- ⚠️ This answers Hume's missing unity at the level of **possible experience**; it does not prove an immortal Cartesian soul or by itself settle every practical question of personal identity through time.
- ❓ The Humean can reply that Kant has stated a condition of **description** rather than discovered an entity; Kant accepts the non-entity point but insists that rule-governed unity is presupposed by judgment.
- ⚠️ **Cross-tradition parallel**, worth one clause: the bundle theory strikingly resembles the Buddhist doctrine of no-self (*nairātmyavāda*), a bridge some examiners reward — but it is a **parallel**, not an influence, and must not be inflated.

#### 5. God — the three fronts, all lost

| Front | Hume's argument | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| **Cosmological / causal** | We have no impression of a "first cause" or of causation as necessary connexion, so we cannot legitimately infer from experienced effects to an unexperienced transcendent cause | The argument Locke used at *Essay* IV is cut off at its premise |
| **Design / teleological** (*Dialogues concerning Natural Religion*) | The analogy — universe : designer :: watch : watchmaker — is weak, because the universe is not sufficiently similar to a manufactured artifact; even if granted it proves at most a **finite** architect working on given matter, not an omnipotent, omniscient, morally perfect creator; order might arise from natural principles without intelligence; and the problem of evil undermines any inference to a perfectly good designer | The design argument survives, if at all, only as a weak analogy |
| **Miracles** (*Enquiry* X) | A miracle violates the laws of nature; testimony in favour of a miracle is always less probable than the testimony being false | It is never rational to believe miracle-reports on testimony alone |

- ⚠️ **Ownership note:** the miracle argument is secondary enrichment here; Hume's **critique of the proofs** is core, because "God" is printed in the syllabus clause.
- ⚠️ **Cross-paper link:** the design-argument critique is also the required content for Paper II questions on proofs for God's existence; the argument is the same, the paper is different.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Hume's bundle theory dissolves the substantial self without denying the reality of experience, and its most important feature is that its author declares it incomplete.
> **Named evidence.** *Treatise* I.iv.6 reports that introspection never delivers a constant and invariable impression but only heat, cold, light, shade, love, hatred, pain and pleasure in perpetual flux, and concludes that the self is a bundle or collection of different perceptions; the Appendix then confesses that all his hopes vanish when he comes to explain the principles that unite them.
> **Analysis.** The unity we experience is produced by resemblance and causation, which let the imagination pass smoothly along the series and mistake an easy transition for an identical object; but because Hume has denied both a substance and any real connexion between distinct existences, no principle of unity remains available to him, so the failure indicts his atomism of impressions rather than the self.
> **Qualification.** Kant's transcendental unity of apperception addresses precisely this gap by treating the "I think" as a formal condition of unified experience rather than as a discovered entity, which is why it is a reply to Hume and not a return to Descartes — though the Humean may fairly answer that a condition of description is not yet the discovery of a subject.

#### Exam link

- **2020 Q2(a), 20 marks** — the quoted sentence is answered by §§1–3, and the Kant limb by §4. The empiricist argument must be complete before Kant appears.
- **2025 P-II** proofs-for-God questions draw directly on §5, and the same material supports any Paper I comparison of God across the three empiricists.
- **2021 Q1(d)** gains its contrast from placing Locke's forensic person beside Hume's bundle.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "Hume says the self does not exist" | He denies a **simple identical self-substance**; the perceptions are real |
| Omitting the Appendix | The retraction is the most valuable single item in this session |
| "Kant proves the soul exists" | Kant supplies a **formal condition**, and explicitly not a soul-substance |
| Making the Buddhist parallel a paragraph | It is a bridge in one clause; the syllabus owner is Indian Philosophy |
| Reporting the design argument as merely "weak analogy" | Give the four separate objections, including the finite-architect point |

#### Revision notes

- No constant impression of a self; only perceptions in perpetual flux.
- *Treatise* I.iv.6: "a bundle or collection of different perceptions".
- Fiction of unity produced by **resemblance** and **causation**.
- Appendix: "all my hopes vanish" — the uniting principle cannot be stated; the *Enquiry* drops the topic.
- Kant: the "I think" must be able to accompany representations; transcendental unity of apperception is **formal**.
- God: cosmological cut at the premise; design weak, at most a finite architect, plus the problem of evil; miracles never rationally credible on testimony.

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — HUME II: THE SELF AND GOD

```text
COPY PRINCIPLE APPLIED TO THE PERCEIVER
        |
        v
INTROSPECTION: only particular perceptions in PERPETUAL FLUX; no constant
and invariable impression of a self
        |
        v
SELF = "A BUNDLE OR COLLECTION OF DIFFERENT PERCEPTIONS" (Treatise I.iv.6)
        |
        +--> WHY IT FEELS UNIFIED: RESEMBLANCE + CAUSATION -> easy transition
        |    mistaken for an identical object -> FICTION of identity
        +--> APPENDIX: "all my hopes vanish" - no uniting principle available
        |    DIAGNOSIS: the ATOMISM of impressions is at fault, not the self
        +--> the Enquiry silently DROPS personal identity
        |
        v
KANT (2020 second limb): accepts there is no soul-substance; the "I THINK"
must be able to accompany all representations; TRANSCENDENTAL UNITY OF
APPERCEPTION = a FORMAL CONDITION, not an entity
   HUMEAN REJOINDER: a condition of description, not a discovered subject
        |
        v
GOD - THREE FRONTS, ALL LOST

   DESIGN (Dialogues): weak analogy | at most a FINITE architect | natural
      order without intelligence | the problem of evil
   MIRACLES (Enquiry X): testimony is always less probable than its falsehood
TRAP: "self does not exist" | omitting the Appendix | Kant as soul-prover
ANSWER LINE: Hume finds the perceptions and loses the owner - and admits it
```

### SESSION 12 — HUME III: CAUSATION, THE TWO DEFINITIONS, THE PROBLEM OF INDUCTION, KANT'S RESPONSE AND MITIGATED SCEPTICISM

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** Watch one billiard ball strike another as often as you like: you will see one move, then the other, always in that order and always touching — but you will never see the pushing itself. Hume says the "must" we feel is a habit inside us that repetition has installed, not a link we have observed in the world.

**Technical definition:** Hume's analysis of causation replaces objective **necessary connexion** with three observable elements — **constant conjunction**, **temporal priority** and **spatial contiguity** — plus an **impression of reflection**, the felt determination of the mind to pass from the idea of the cause to that of the effect; from this follow his **two definitions of cause**, the sceptical **problem of induction**, and the positive doctrine of **mitigated scepticism**.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Hume does not deny causation; he **relocates necessity** — from the objects, where no impression of a power or tie is ever given, to the observer, in whom repetition installs a felt determination to pass from cause to effect — and because that relocation converts the foundation of every factual inference into a habit, it produces the problem of induction, which is why the same doctrine answers a question about causation and a question about the limits of reason.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **constant conjunction, temporal priority, contiguity** — the three observables
- **no impression of necessary connexion** — the negative result
- **impression of reflection** — the felt determination of the mind
- **custom or habit** — "the great guide of human life" (*Enquiry* V)
- **two definitions of cause** — the philosophical and the natural
- **the Uniformity Principle** — and the dilemma that traps it
- **mitigated scepticism** versus **excessive or Pyrrhonian scepticism**

**How to use them:** run the negative argument in numbered steps, produce **both** definitions, then run induction as a formal dilemma, then Kant's Second Analogy, then close on mitigated scepticism. Naming both definitions is the single highest-value move available in this sub-topic.

#### Plain-language visual — what you actually see, and what you add

```text
   THE BILLIARD TABLE                                  Enquiry IV, VII
   +------------------------------------------------------------------+
   |  BALL A moves ---> touches BALL B ---> BALL B moves               |
   |                                                                   |
   |  WHAT THE SENSES DELIVER:                                         |
   |     PRIORITY    A moves first                                     |
   |     CONTIGUITY  they touch                                        |
   |     CONSTANT    every time so far, B has followed A               |
   |     CONJUNCTION                                                   |
   |                                                                   |
   |  WHAT THE SENSES NEVER DELIVER:                                   |
   |     the POWER, the FORCE, the TIE, the "must"                     |
   +------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                |
   REPETITION ADDS NOTHING TO THE OBJECTS. The hundredth collision is
   qualitatively no different from the first, as far as the balls go.
                                |
   BUT REPETITION CHANGES *ME*. After constant conjunction, my mind is
   DETERMINED to pass from the idea of A to the idea of B. THAT felt
   determination is an IMPRESSION OF REFLECTION - and the idea of
   necessary connexion is copied from it.
                                |
   CONCLUSION: necessity "is something that exists in the mind, not in
   objects." Causation is not denied; NECESSITY IS RELOCATED.
```

**In simple words:** the argument is an audit, exactly like the audits of substance and self. Name the idea, demand its impression, search outside, search inside, and follow the search where it goes. Hume even checks the most promising internal case — the will moving a limb — and finds no impression of the mechanism there either.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Observed | priority, contiguity, constant conjunction |
| Not observed | power, force, necessary connexion |
| Source of the idea | an **impression of reflection**: the felt determination of the mind |
| Mechanism | **custom or habit**, not rational demonstration |
| Result | necessity is *projected onto* objects, not read off them |
| Not the result | that causal inference should stop; nature compels it |

> **One-line memory:** the necessity is real — it is just in the **spectator**, not in the **collision**.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the two arguments, the two definitions and the dilemma

```text
   (A) THE NEGATIVE ARGUMENT ON NECESSARY CONNEXION  Treatise I.iii.14;
                                                      Enquiry VII
   1 COPY PRINCIPLE: every simple idea derives from a simple impression.
   2 So if we have a genuine idea of NECESSARY CONNEXION, locate its
     source impression.
   3 EXTERNAL SEARCH: in any single instance of causation, sense delivers
     only priority, contiguity and the succession of qualities. No
     impression of a POWER or TIE is given.
   4 Repetition adds no new quality TO THE OBJECTS: the hundredth
     collision is qualitatively no different from the first.
   5 INTERNAL SEARCH: volition does no better. I have no impression of
     HOW the will moves the limb; nerve and muscle are unknown to
     consciousness. This rules out the Cartesian and the occasionalist
     appeal alike.
   6 But repetition DOES change THE MIND: after constant conjunction the
     mind is DETERMINED to pass from the idea of A to the idea of B.
     That felt determination is an IMPRESSION OF REFLECTION.
   7 THEREFORE the idea of necessary connexion is copied from that
     internal impression. Necessity "is something that exists in the
     mind, not in objects."
   8 THEREFORE TWO DEFINITIONS OF CAUSE FOLLOW:
     +--------------------------------+-------------------------------+
     | (i) OBJECTIVE / PHILOSOPHICAL  | (ii) SUBJECTIVE / NATURAL     |
     | an object followed by another, | an object followed by another,|
     | where all objects similar to   | whose appearance CONVEYS THE  |
     | the first are followed by      | THOUGHT to the other          |
     | objects similar to the second  |                               |
     | regularity, external           | psychological, internal       |
     +--------------------------------+-------------------------------+
     NAMING BOTH IS THE HIGHEST-VALUE MOVE IN ANY HUME-ON-CAUSATION ANSWER.
     TENSION worth flagging: the two pull apart and Hume never reconciles
     them - the origin of the modern regularity-versus-projectivist debate.
   PRESUPPOSITIONS: P1 the copy principle is exceptionless; P2 single and
     repeated instances can be compared for ADDED content; P3 impressions
     are atomic and separable, so no relation is given WITH its terms;
     P4 "necessity" must be either observed or projected - no third option
     such as an inferred theoretical posit.
   CANONICAL EXAMPLES: the two billiard balls (Enquiry IV, VII); bread
     nourishing the body (Enquiry IV) - the sensible qualities of bread
     give no clue to its nutritive power.
   =====================================================================
   (B) THE PROBLEM OF INDUCTION AS A FORMAL DILEMMA          Enquiry IV
   1 All reasoning about matters of fact beyond present sense and memory
     rests on the relation of cause and effect.
   2 Causal inference rests on the UNIFORMITY PRINCIPLE (UP): the future
     will resemble the past; unobserved cases resemble observed ones.
   3 UP can be established only DEMONSTRATIVELY or PROBABLY. <-- THE FORK
     APPLIED REFLEXIVELY; this exhaustive division is the vulnerable step.
   4 NOT DEMONSTRATIVELY: "the course of nature may change" implies no
     contradiction - it is perfectly conceivable.
   5 NOT PROBABLY: every probable or experiential argument already
     PRESUPPOSES UP, so the argument would be circular.
   6 THEREFORE UP has no rational foundation.
   7 THEREFORE inductive inference is produced not by reason but by
     CUSTOM OR HABIT - "the great guide of human life" (Enquiry V).
   EVERY SERIOUS REPLY ATTACKS STEP 3:
     KANT supplies a THIRD category - the synthetic a priori
     POPPER denies that science needs induction at all
     STRAWSON argues that demanding a justification of induction AS A
       WHOLE misapplies the very standards induction constitutes
   =====================================================================
   (C) KANT'S RESPONSE - the routed reply in 2025 Q2(c)
   - Kant AGREES that necessary connection is not copied from a sensory
     impression.
   - SECOND ANALOGY: the category of cause is an A PRIORI RULE required to
     distinguish OBJECTIVE event-succession from the merely SUBJECTIVE
     succession of my perceptions.
   - "Every event has a cause" is therefore SYNTHETIC A PRIORI for
     possible experience: the understanding supplies necessary temporal
     ordering to phenomena.
   - LIMIT: this rescues objective empirical causation WITHOUT claiming
     knowledge of causality among things in themselves.
   - HUMEAN CHALLENGE: has Kant PROVED the category, or BUILT it into the
     definition of objective experience? KANT'S REPLY is transcendental -
     without such rule-governed synthesis, experience of an EVENT would
     not be possible at all.
   =====================================================================
   (D) MITIGATED SCEPTICISM - the positive doctrine        Enquiry XII
   EXCESSIVE / PYRRHONIAN: total suspension of judgment - UNLIVEABLE
   MITIGATED: limit enquiry to what experience can answer; avoid
     speculation beyond possible experience; proportion belief to
     evidence; accept the natural beliefs custom compels
```

⚠️ **Caption.** Diagram (A) step 5 is the step scripts omit. Hume does not merely say we see no power in billiard balls; he closes the *internal* escape route, and that closure is what makes the conclusion general rather than anecdotal.

#### 1. The elements, tabulated

| Element | Hume's analysis |
|---|---|
| What we observe | constant conjunction (A always followed by B) + temporal priority (A precedes B) + spatial contiguity |
| What we do **not** observe | a *necessary connexion* between cause and effect |
| Source of the idea of necessary connexion | not any impression of objects; an **impression of reflection** — a *feeling* in the mind, a determination to pass from the idea of A to the idea of B after repeated experience |
| Mechanism | **custom or habit**: after repeated observation of A → B the mind *expects* B on seeing A; this expectation is a **psychological compulsion**, not a rational demonstration |
| Conclusion | causal necessity is not *in* objects; it is **projected onto** them by the mind's habitual association |

#### 2. Is there any element of necessity in causal relations? — the 2019 answer in two lines

- ✅ **In objects themselves — NO.** There is no impression of objective necessity.
- ✅ **In the mind — YES**, in a psychological sense: the impression of reflection, the felt compulsion to expect B after A, is a kind of **subjective necessity**. But this is a fact about *us*, not about *nature*.
- ⚠️ The directive in 2019 Q4(b) is "Is there any element…?", which demands a **yes-and-no with a distinction**, never a flat "no". Answer in the first line, then explain.

#### 3. The two definitions — and why both must appear

- ✅ **(i) Objective or philosophical:** an object followed by another, where all objects similar to the first are followed by objects similar to the second.
- ✅ **(ii) Subjective or natural:** an object followed by another, whose appearance **conveys the thought** to the other.
- ⚠️ The two pull apart: the first is regularity-based and external, the second psychological and internal, and Hume never reconciles them. That unreconciled tension is the origin of the modern debate between regularity and projectivist accounts of causation, and naming it turns exposition into assessment.

#### 4. Induction — the dilemma, and where every reply must strike

The seven steps are set out in diagram (B). The examinable point is that **step 3 is the vulnerable one**: it assumes the exhaustiveness of the demonstrative/probable dichotomy, which is Hume's own fork applied reflexively. Kant breaks that horn with the synthetic a priori; Popper denies science needs induction; Strawson dissolves the demand.

> ⚠️ **Executable verdict on induction:** the sceptical conclusion depends on the exhaustiveness of a dichotomy that is itself the fork, so Hume's scepticism about induction is not a discovery about the world but the shadow cast by his classification of propositions — which is exactly why it survives only as long as the fork does.

#### 5. Kant's response, and how far it goes

- ✅ Kant **agrees** that necessary connection is not copied from a sensory impression — so he is not contradicting Hume's negative result, he is accepting it.
- ✅ In the **Second Analogy** he argues that the category of cause is an *a priori* rule required to distinguish **objective** event-succession from the merely **subjective** succession of perceptions. Without a rule, the difference between a ship drifting downstream and my eye wandering over a house cannot be drawn.
- ✅ "Every event has a cause" is therefore **synthetic a priori** for possible experience: the understanding supplies necessary temporal ordering to phenomena.
- ⚠️ Kant rescues objective empirical causation **without** claiming knowledge of causality among things in themselves.
- ❓ Hume can challenge whether Kant proves the category or builds it into the definition of objective experience; the Kantian reply is transcendental — without such rule-governed synthesis, experience of an *event* would not be possible.
- ⚠️ **Ownership discipline:** Kant is the routed reply, not the doctrine under examination. In 2025 Q2(c) the Hume limb must be complete before the Kant limb begins.

#### 6. Mitigated scepticism — the positive recommendation

- ✅ Hume does **not** recommend suspending all belief; that would be unliveable. He distinguishes:
  - **Excessive or Pyrrhonian scepticism** — total suspension of judgment, impossible in practice;
  - **Mitigated scepticism** — a cautious, modest attitude: limit enquiry to what experience can answer, avoid speculation about things beyond possible experience (substance, God, the soul), and proportion belief to evidence.
- ✅ This is Hume's positive doctrine: philosophical humility plus reliance on the natural beliefs custom compels.
- ❓ **The standing objection:** mitigated scepticism looks inconsistent, since Hume *uses* induction while denying its rational basis. **His reply:** nature forces belief on us; philosophy reveals its groundlessness; both can be true without practical contradiction. ⚠️ The position is stable **only if** natural beliefs are exempt from the demand for justification — which concedes that not all belief needs reason.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Hume's account of causation relocates necessity from the world to the observer, and the problem of induction is the immediate consequence rather than a separate scepticism.
> **Named evidence.** *Treatise* I.iii.14 and *Enquiry* VII search for an impression of necessary connexion, find only priority, contiguity and constant conjunction in the billiard-ball case, close the internal route by noting that we have no impression of how the will moves a limb, and locate the idea in the mind's determination to pass from cause to effect, yielding the two definitions of cause; *Enquiry* IV then shows that the Uniformity Principle can be established neither demonstratively, since its denial implies no contradiction, nor probably, since every experiential argument presupposes it.
> **Analysis.** Because the very habit that produces the idea of necessity is also the mechanism of all factual inference, the analysis of causation and the problem of induction are one result stated twice, and custom becomes the great guide of human life precisely where reason has been shown to be silent.
> **Qualification.** The result follows from the atomism of impressions rather than from experience itself — if perceptions are separable atoms, no relation can be perceived and the conclusion is guaranteed by the premise — so Kant's Second Analogy is correctly aimed when it attacks the atomism, though the Humean may still ask whether the category has been proved or presupposed.

#### Exam link

- **2023 Q2(a), 20 marks** — "Critically analyse Hume's argument that causality is a matter of habit/custom involving psychological principle of association." Diagram (A) plus §3 plus the objections; "critically analyse" demands a verdict.
- **2021 Q1(e), 10 marks** — "constant conjunction" is the quoted phrase; the answer is §1 plus the impression of reflection.
- **2019 Q4(b), 15 marks** — §2 is the whole hinge: not in objects, yes in the mind.
- **2025 Q2(c), 15 marks** — §§1–3 then §5, in that order.
- **2018 Q1(a), 10 marks** — the fork from Session 10 plus §4's conceivability point.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "Hume denies causation" | He denies the **idea of necessary connexion in objects**; causal inference continues, compelled by custom |
| "Hume's causation = mere succession" | Constant conjunction **plus** priority **plus** contiguity **plus** the felt necessity |
| Giving only one definition of cause | Both are required; naming both is the highest-value move here |
| Answering 2019 with a flat "no" | The question asks "any element", so distinguish objects from mind |
| Treating Kant as refuting Hume's premise | Kant **accepts** the negative result and attacks the atomism instead |
| Calling Hume a Pyrrhonian | His scepticism is explicitly **mitigated** |

#### Revision notes

- Observed: constant conjunction, priority, contiguity. Not observed: power or tie.
- Internal search fails too: no impression of how the will moves the limb.
- Idea of necessity copied from an **impression of reflection**; mechanism is **custom or habit**.
- Two definitions: regularity-based (philosophical) and thought-conveying (natural); unreconciled.
- Induction: UP not demonstrable (denial conceivable), not probable (circular) → custom.
- Step 3 of the dilemma is the target of Kant, Popper and Strawson.
- Kant: Second Analogy; cause is an a priori rule; synthetic a priori for possible experience; no claim about things in themselves.
- Mitigated, not Pyrrhonian; stable only if natural beliefs are exempt from the demand for justification.

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — HUME III: CAUSATION, INDUCTION AND SCEPTICISM

```text
DEMAND THE IMPRESSION OF "NECESSARY CONNEXION"
        |
        +--> EXTERNAL SEARCH: only PRIORITY + CONTIGUITY + CONSTANT CONJUNCTION
        +--> REPETITION adds nothing to the OBJECTS
        +--> INTERNAL SEARCH: no impression of how the will moves the limb
        |
        v
FOUND ONLY: the MIND'S DETERMINATION to pass A -> B = IMPRESSION OF REFLECTION
   NECESSITY IS RELOCATED: in the mind, not in objects
        |
        +--> TWO DEFINITIONS OF CAUSE
        |    (i) philosophical: regular succession of similars
        |    (ii) natural: appearance CONVEYS THE THOUGHT to the other
        |    they pull apart and are never reconciled
        |
        v
INDUCTION AS A DILEMMA (Enquiry IV)
   UP: the future resembles the past
   not DEMONSTRABLE (denial conceivable) | not PROBABLE (circular)
   -> no rational foundation -> CUSTOM, "the great guide of human life"
   ATTACK POINT: step 3, the demonstrative/probable dichotomy = the FORK
   KANT third category | POPPER no induction needed | STRAWSON dissolve demand
        |
        v
KANT'S SECOND ANALOGY: cause is an A PRIORI RULE distinguishing objective
event-succession from subjective succession; synthetic a priori for possible
experience; silent about things in themselves
        |
        v
MITIGATED SCEPTICISM (Enquiry XII): not Pyrrhonian suspension but bounded
enquiry, proportioned belief and reliance on natural belief
TRAP: "denies causation" | mere succession | one definition | flat "no" in 2019
ANSWER LINE: necessity is real, but it lives in the spectator
```

### SESSION 13 — COMPARISON, CRITICISMS AND REPLIES, ANSWER ARCHITECTURE, DIRECTIVES AND PROVENANCE

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** This session turns the three systems into one comparison table, lists what each thinker's critics say and what he can answer, and then converts all of it into the sentences and structures that actually earn marks in the examination hall.

**Technical definition:** A synoptic treatment of British empiricism on **matched axes** — matter, qualities, substance, self, causation, God, scepticism and overall position — together with a criticism-reply-residual ledger for each thinker, graded verdicts at 10, 15 and 20 marks, a directive decoder, and the citation and provenance rules that protect factual accuracy.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Comparative questions in this item are lost by scripts that write three summaries and won by scripts that announce a **criterion** first: choose one axis — what each thinker will admit as an object of knowledge — and Locke's represented matter, Berkeley's ideas and spirits, and Hume's perceptions arrange themselves in a single argument, so that every subsequent difference about substance, self, causation and God is a consequence rather than a separate paragraph.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **matched axes** — the same question asked three times
- **criterion announced first** — the structural move that earns the comparison mark
- **criticism → reply → residual** — the three-column discipline
- **directive fidelity** — "examine", "critically analyse", "is there any…?"
- **provenance mark** — naming what is a later label and what is the author's own words
- **graded verdict** — a ruling scaled to the marks available

**How to use them:** open with the criterion, run the axes, then rule. Use the criticism ledger for the middle of any 15- or 20-marker, and close with a graded verdict rather than a summary.

#### Plain-language visual — the same four questions, asked three times

```text
   ASK EACH MAN THE SAME FOUR QUESTIONS AND THE STORY TELLS ITSELF
   +-----------------+-------------+---------------+-----------------+
   | QUESTION        | LOCKE       | BERKELEY      | HUME            |
   +-----------------+-------------+---------------+-----------------+
   | Is there matter | yes, but    | no - the word | no impression,  |
   | behind the      | its nature  | has no        | so no clear     |
   | qualities?      | is unknown  | content       | idea at all     |
   +-----------------+-------------+---------------+-----------------+
   | Is there a self | yes: a      | yes: an       | no: a bundle    |
   | that owns the   | continuing  | ACTIVE spirit | in perpetual    |
   | experiences?    | consciousness| known by      | flux            |
   |                 |             | notion        |                 |
   +-----------------+-------------+---------------+-----------------+
   | Is there real   | yes - real  | yes - but the | no - constant   |
   | causal power?   | power, with | only agent is | conjunction     |
   |                 | limited     | SPIRIT; laws  | plus habit      |
   |                 | knowledge   | are SIGNS     |                 |
   +-----------------+-------------+---------------+-----------------+
   | Can God be      | yes, by     | yes - and he  | no - every      |
   | proved?         | DEMONSTRA-  | is REQUIRED   | proof fails     |
   |                 | TION        | by the system |                 |
   +-----------------+-------------+---------------+-----------------+
              |                |                    |
         MODERATE          THEISTIC              MITIGATED
         REALISM           IDEALISM              SCEPTICISM
```

**In simple words:** a comparison is an argument, not a gallery. If you can say in one line *why* Berkeley must keep spirit while Hume need not, you have shown the examiner the mechanism, and the table then reads as evidence rather than as decoration.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Axis | one question asked of all three |
| Criterion | the standard by which you will rank them |
| Residual | what remains true after the reply is given |
| Directive fidelity | doing exactly what the command word demands |
| Provenance | separating an author's words from the tradition's labels |
| Graded verdict | a ruling proportioned to the marks |

> **One-line memory:** announce the **criterion**, run the **axes**, then **rule**.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the eight-axis comparison and the criticism ledger

```text
   THE EIGHT-AXIS COMPARISON
   =====================================================================
   AXIS         | LOCKE            | BERKELEY          | HUME
   -------------+------------------+-------------------+-----------------
   MATTER       | real but         | does not exist    | no impression ->
                | unknowable       | (a fiction/       | fiction
                | substrate        | meaningless word) |
   QUALITIES    | primary          | ALL mind-         | all are
                | (objective) vs   | dependent; the    | impressions; no
                | secondary        | distinction       | basis for an
                | (subjective)     | COLLAPSES         | objective/
                |                  |                   | subjective split
   SUBSTANCE    | "something I     | spirit only (no   | no substance at
                | know not what"   | material          | all - material
                |                  | substance)        | or spiritual
   SELF         | continuity of    | active spirit     | bundle of
                | consciousness    | (real), known by  | perceptions
                | (memory)         | NOTION            | (no self)
   CAUSATION    | real power       | God's will        | constant
                | (knowledge of    | producing ideas   | conjunction +
                | mechanism        | in us             | habit (no real
                | limited)         |                   | power)
   GOD          | demonstrated by  | required to       | all proofs fail;
                | a cosmological-  | sustain           | scepticism
                | style argument   | unperceived ideas |
   SCEPTICISM   | rejected         | claims to REFUTE  | embraced, but
                | (moderate        | scepticism        | MITIGATED
                | realism)         |                   |
   OVERALL      | representative   | subjective        | phenomenalism +
                | realism          | (theistic)        | scepticism
                |                  | idealism          |
   =====================================================================
   THE CRITICISM LEDGER - CRITICISM | SOURCE | REPLY | RESIDUAL
   ---------------------------------------------------------------------
   AGAINST LOCKE
   1 The primary/secondary distinction is unstable - can extension be
     conceived without any sensory quality?            SOURCE: Berkeley
     REPLY: we can THINK extension abstractly through mathematical
     reasoning even if we always IMAGINE it with colour.
     RESIDUAL: the reply needs a non-imagistic idea, which strains
     Locke's own theory of ideas.
   2 Representative realism generates scepticism - how can ideas be
     verified against reality?          SOURCE: Berkeley and sceptics
     REPLY: God would not make us systematically deceived; inference to
     external causes is reliable enough for the conduct of life.
     RESIDUAL: this converts an epistemological problem into a
     theological guarantee.
   3 Personal identity via memory is circular.   SOURCE: Butler, Reid
     REPLY: distinguish the CONSTITUTIVE criterion (consciousness) from
     the EVIDENTIAL criterion (memory); repair transitivity with
     overlapping chains.
     RESIDUAL: "consciousness of it AS MINE" may re-import ownership.
   ---------------------------------------------------------------------
   AGAINST BERKELEY
   4 Common sense: surely tables exist unperceived.
     SOURCE: reported in Samuel Johnson's stone-kicking - RECEPTION only
     REPLY: the table DOES exist unperceived by you - God perceives it.
     The theory SAVES common sense rather than destroying it.
     RESIDUAL: continuity is bought with a theological premise.
   5 Solipsism threat: if reality is my ideas, what of other minds?
     REPLY: other spirits are known by ANALOGY; God guarantees the
     whole system.
     RESIDUAL: knowledge of other spirits is less immediate than
     awareness of one's own activity.
   6 Why invoke God at all? This is dogmatic, not empiricist. SOURCE: Hume
     REPLY: Berkeley accepts that he is a theist using empiricist methods
     to defend theism.
     RESIDUAL: God does exactly the work Locke's matter did.
   7 How does subjective idealism differ from Hegel's absolute idealism?
     SOURCE: the 2024 examiner
     REPLY: Berkeley - reality is ideas in finite and divine minds;
     Hegel - reality is the self-development of Absolute Spirit.
     RESIDUAL: the two are different in KIND, not degree.
   ---------------------------------------------------------------------
   AGAINST HUME
   8 If there is no real causal power, science becomes inexplicable.
     SOURCE: Kant and scientists
     REPLY: science works by INDUCTIVE HABIT, not by rational insight
     into necessity; its success is a psychological fact, not a
     philosophical justification.
     RESIDUAL: an account of success is not an account of warrant.
   9 What holds the bundle together?   SOURCE: critics; HUME HIMSELF
     REPLY: none is offered - "all my hopes vanish" (Appendix).
     RESIDUAL: the failure indicts the atomism, not the self.
   10 Self-refutation: is the FORK itself a relation of ideas or a
     matter of fact? If neither, it burns itself.       SOURCE: critics
     REPLY: it is a clarification of the meanings of terms - a
     meta-level classification, not a first-order claim.
     RESIDUAL: the status of the meta-level remains contested.
   11 Mitigated scepticism is inconsistent: Hume USES induction while
     denying its rational basis.
     REPLY: nature forces belief; philosophy reveals its groundlessness;
     both can be true without practical contradiction.
     RESIDUAL: stable only if natural beliefs are exempt from the demand
     for justification.
```

⚠️ **Caption.** Use the ledger three columns at a time. A criticism without a reply is journalism; a reply without a residual is advocacy; only the third column shows judgement.

#### 1. Why the empiricist sequence "slides" — the mechanism restated

```text
LOCKE   : all ideas from experience -> matter is real but unknowable
             |  Berkeley asks: if all we know is ideas, what grounds
             |  belief in MATTER?
BERKELEY: no impression of matter -> drop material substance
             |  Hume asks: apply the same razor to the SELF, to
             |  SUBSTANCE, to CAUSATION
HUME    : no impression of self, no impression of necessary connexion
          -> bundle, habit, mitigated scepticism
```

⚠️ Each step is the **previous thinker's premise taken more consistently**. This is the master narrative of the whole item, and it is what provokes Kant to rescue knowledge by adding the *a priori* contribution of the mind.

#### 2. Directive decoder — what each command word obliges you to do

| Directive | What it demands | Structural obligation in this item | Fatal error |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Explain / Elucidate** (2025 Q4c, 2021 Q1c) | make the doctrine intelligible by exhibiting its *derivation* | give the numbered argument — Berkeley's five, Hume's two — not the conclusion | restating *esse est percipi* or "constant conjunction" in synonyms |
| **Examine** (2021 Q1d) | state, then test | doctrine **and** objection, then a ruling on the objection's force | describing the doctrine and stopping |
| **Critically examine / Critically discuss** | assessment dominates | three or four lines of reconstruction, then objection → reply → **adjudication** | listing objections without ruling |
| **Critically analyse** (2023 Q2a) | exposition **plus** a verdict | both definitions of cause must appear, then the objections, then the ruling | the negative half only |
| **Discuss** (2019 Q4b, 2018 Q1a) | exposition plus assessment, roughly 60:40 | for causation, both Humean definitions; for the fork, both boxes | a one-sided report |
| **Is there any element …?** (2019 Q4b) | a **yes-and-no with a distinction** | *not in objects; yes in the mind*, then the impression of reflection | a flat "no" |
| **Comment on the statement** (quotation-led parts) | locate the sentence in its own text and system first | name the work and section, state the thesis it encodes, then two consequences, then a judgment | a generic essay on the thinker |
| **How does X refute Y?** (2022 Q4c) | a reconstruction of the refuting argument, premise by premise | Locke's three grounds **and** the dispositional reply | listing Locke's points only |
| **Is A instrumental in B?** (2024 Q2a) | a causal-explanatory verdict | show *which* rejection does the work — likeness and abstraction, not relativity | narrating both doctrines separately |

#### 3. Graded verdict bank — executable closing positions

| Sub-topic | 10-mark verdict | 15-mark verdict | 20-mark verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Innate ideas** | Locke defeats the naive innatist thesis of universally conscious innate content, but not the dispositional version Descartes actually held | …Locke's decisive premise is that nothing can be in the mind unperceived; that premise, not the facts about children, does the work — and it is a stipulation | The dispute is not empirical but about what "in the mind" means. Locke wins the debate as staged and loses it as reformulated; innatism survives as a thesis about **structure**, which is precisely the form Kant vindicates |
