##### Demand decoding

| Element | Requirement |
|---|---|
| Directive | "Examine" — state **and** test; a ruling on the objections is required |
| Core issue | Whether continuity of consciousness can constitute identity |
| Scope boundary | Ten marks: three identities, the criterion, one case, two objections, one verdict |
| Ideal thesis | Forensically indispensable, metaphysically incomplete |
| Non-negotiables | Person/man/substance; II.xxvii.9; appropriation; forensic; prince and cobbler; Butler–Reid; Brave Officer |

##### Independent model answer

Locke separates three questions his predecessors ran together: sameness of **substance**, sameness of **man** (the continuing living organism), and sameness of **person**. Only the third is his subject.

A person is "a thinking intelligent being, that has reason and reflection, and can consider itself as itself, the same thinking thing, in different times and places" (*Essay* II.xxvii.9, added in the second edition of 1694). Identity of person therefore extends as far as present **consciousness** can **appropriate** a past thought or action as its own. Memory is the vehicle and evidence of that reach, not the definition. "Person" is, in Locke's own word, **forensic**: it is built for accountability.

The prince-and-cobbler case makes the separation vivid: transfer the prince's consciousness and the **person** who wakes is the prince while the **man** is the cobbler.

Two objections press hard. Butler and Reid charge **circularity**, since I can remember only my own experiences. Reid's **Brave Officer** shows the criterion is not transitive: the officer remembers the boy, the general remembers the officer, the general does not remember the boy.

**Verdict.** ⚠️ Repaired as overlapping chains of consciousness, the theory survives Reid; the circularity charge remains open. Forensically indispensable, metaphysically incomplete.

##### Why this earns marks

**Answer-writing focus — 2021 Q1(d):** "Examine" is a two-part instruction. Doctrine alone, however accurate, caps the mark.

**Exam-length execution — 10 marks, about 150 to 200 words:** three identities in two lines; definition and criterion in four; the case in two; objections in three; verdict in one.

- **Structure:** three identities → definition → criterion → case → objections → graded verdict.
- **Doctrine:** consciousness, appropriation, forensic concept.
- **Evidence:** II.xxvii.9; the 1694 addition; prince and cobbler; the Brave Officer.
- **Distinction:** constitutive criterion versus evidential criterion.
- **Criticism:** the repair costs the simple memory reading.

#### 2021 · Q1(e) · 10 marks

**Question:** “The relation between cause and effect is one of constant conjunction”. Examine Hume’s criticism of causation in the light of the above statement.

##### Demand decoding

| Element | Requirement |
|---|---|
| Directive | "Examine … in the light of the statement" — the quoted phrase must be tested, not merely repeated |
| Core issue | Whether constant conjunction exhausts the analysis |
| Scope boundary | Ten marks: the three observables, the missing power, the internal find, one qualification |
| Ideal thesis | Constant conjunction is necessary but not sufficient; the felt determination completes the account |
| Non-negotiables | Priority, contiguity, constant conjunction; no impression of power; impression of reflection; custom |

##### Independent model answer

The quoted sentence is accurate but incomplete, and examining it is the answer.

Hume's analysis begins with what observation supplies: temporal **priority**, spatial **contiguity** and, across repeated instances, **constant conjunction**. What observation never supplies is a **power** or **tie** binding cause to effect. Applying the copy principle, Hume asks which impression the idea of necessary connexion is copied from. No external impression answers, and repetition adds nothing to the objects themselves.

The internal route also fails: I have no impression of how the will moves a limb.

What repetition does alter is the mind. After constant conjunction it is **determined** to pass from the idea of the cause to that of the effect, and this felt determination is an **impression of reflection** from which the idea of necessity is copied. Causal necessity is therefore projected onto objects, not read off them, and the mechanism is **custom or habit**.

**Verdict.** ⚠️ Constant conjunction alone is mere regularity; Hume's account requires priority, contiguity **and** the felt determination. He does not deny causation — he relocates its necessity from the world to the observer.

##### Why this earns marks

**Answer-writing focus — 2021 Q1(e):** the examiner supplies a partial formula. The mark is for showing what it omits.

**Exam-length execution — 10 marks, about 150 to 200 words:** the three observables in three lines; the failed searches in four; the impression of reflection in three; verdict in two.

- **Structure:** what is observed → what is not → the internal search → the positive find → verdict.
- **Doctrine:** copy principle; constant conjunction; impression of reflection; custom.
- **Evidence:** the billiard-ball case; *Treatise* I.iii.14; *Enquiry* VII.
- **Distinction:** regularity versus the full analysis including felt necessity.
- **Criticism:** "mere succession" is the misreading the quoted line invites.

#### 2022 · Q4(c) · 15 marks

**Question:** Why and how does John Locke refute the innate ideas? Elucidate the nature and source of knowledge in Locke’s epistemology.

##### Demand decoding

| Element | Requirement |
|---|---|
| Directive | Two limbs: **why and how** he refutes, then **elucidate** nature and source of knowledge |
| Core issue | The three grounds and the positive replacement they make room for |
| Scope boundary | Fifteen marks: motive, three grounds, two fountains, simple/complex, degrees, one qualification |
| Ideal thesis | The refutation clears space for a genetic account; it defeats naive but not dispositional innatism |
| Non-negotiables | Universal assent; no unconscious ideas; redundancy; II.i.2; sensation and reflection; the three degrees |

##### Independent model answer

**Why.** Locke's target is the claim that certain ideas and principles are stamped on the mind at birth. If true, it would license appeals to authority that no experience could check, and it would make an account of the *origin* of ideas unnecessary. Locke wants the origin of an idea to be the test of its legitimacy, so the innatist hypothesis must go first.

**How.** Three grounds, in Book I. First, **universal assent fails**: if such ideas were innate, all human beings including children would possess them, and they do not. Second, **there are no unconscious ideas**: a thought the mind does not think is self-contradictory, since to be in the mind is to be perceived by the mind. Third, a **genetic account is available**: every alleged innate idea, including God and non-contradiction, can be traced to experience, so the hypothesis is redundant.

**The positive account.** The mind is "white paper, void of all characters" (II.i.2) — the doctrine traditionally labelled *tabula rasa*, though the Latin is not Locke's. Experience has two channels: **sensation**, giving ideas of external objects, and **reflection**, giving ideas of the mind's own operations. **Simple** ideas are passively received; the understanding actively forms **complex** ideas as modes, substances and relations.

Knowledge is then the **perception of the agreement or disagreement of ideas**, in three degrees: **intuitive** (immediate), **demonstrative** (through intervening ideas) and **sensitive** (of particular existing things). Everything below is probability and judgement.

**Verdict.** ⚠️ Locke defeats naive innatism decisively; against Descartes' **dispositional** innatism his empirical grounds miss, and everything rests on the premise about what "in the mind" means — a stipulation rather than a discovery.

##### Why this earns marks

**Answer-writing focus — 2022 Q4(c):** two limbs, two headings. The second limb is not an afterthought: "nature and source" means the definition of knowledge **and** its two fountains.

**Exam-length execution — 15 marks, about 250 to 300 words:** motive in three lines; three grounds in six; the positive account in six; the degrees in three; verdict in two.

- **Structure:** why → three grounds → sources → nature and degrees → verdict.
- **Doctrine:** anti-innatism; sensation and reflection; simple and complex ideas; the three degrees.
- **Evidence:** *Essay* Book I; II.i.2; the classification at II.xii.
- **Distinction:** naive versus dispositional innatism.
- **Criticism:** the decisive ground is a stipulation about the meaning of "idea".

#### 2023 · Q2(a) · 20 marks

**Question:** Critically analyse Hume’s argument that causality is a matter of habit/custom involving psychological principle of association.

##### Demand decoding

| Element | Requirement |
|---|---|
| Directive | "Critically analyse" — exposition **plus** a delivered verdict |
| Core issue | Whether reducing causal necessity to habit is an explanation or a capitulation |
| Scope boundary | Twenty marks: full analysis, both definitions, association, three objections, ruling |
| Ideal thesis | Hume succeeds against experience-based grounding of necessity; the result follows from his atomism |
| Non-negotiables | Three observables; failed internal search; impression of reflection; both definitions; practice versus justification |

##### Independent model answer

Causation is the hinge on which empiricism's critique of metaphysics and science's rational basis both turn, so the argument deserves close statement before assessment.

**The analysis.** Observation of one event producing another delivers temporal **priority**, spatial **contiguity** and **constant conjunction**. It never delivers a power or tie. Applying the copy principle, Hume looks for the impression from which the idea of necessary connexion could be copied. The external search fails, and repetition adds no new quality to the objects. The internal search fails too: I have no impression of how volition moves a limb, which closes the Cartesian and occasionalist routes at once.

**The positive account.** Repetition changes the observer. After constant conjunction the mind is **determined** to pass from cause to effect; that determination is an **impression of reflection**, and the idea of necessity is copied from it. The mechanism is the **association** of ideas by resemblance, contiguity and causation, and the resulting expectation is **custom**, "the great guide of human life". Two definitions of cause follow — one objective and regularity-based, one psychological, conveying the thought to the other — and Hume never reconciles them.

**Assessment.** Three objections press. **Kant** replies that causality is an *a priori* rule required to distinguish objective succession from the subjective succession of perceptions, so necessity is contributed by the understanding rather than observed in objects. **Pragmatically**, the success of science suggests a real causal order — though Hume answers that success is a psychological fact about creatures built to expect, not a justification. **Self-referentially**, if inference is mere habit, Hume's own arguments lose their force; his defence is that he distinguishes the **practice** of inference, which nature compels, from its **justification**, which reason cannot supply.

**Verdict.** ⚠️ Hume establishes that experience alone cannot ground causal necessity. But the result follows from the **atomism of impressions**: if perceptions are separable atoms, no relation can be perceived, and the conclusion is guaranteed by the premise. Kant's reply is therefore correctly aimed, since it attacks the atomism rather than the inference — while Hume's sceptical result stands as a permanent constraint on dogmatic metaphysics.

##### Why this earns marks

**Answer-writing focus — 2023 Q2(a):** "critically analyse" requires a ruling. Exposition alone, however complete, cannot reach the top band.

**Exam-length execution — 20 marks, about 340 to 400 words:** frame in two lines; analysis in seven; positive account in six; three objections in six; verdict in four.

- **Structure:** frame → analysis → positive account → three objections → ruling.
- **Doctrine:** copy principle; association; custom; two definitions.
- **Evidence:** billiard balls and bread; *Treatise* I.iii.14; *Enquiry* IV, V, VII.
- **Distinction:** the practice of causal inference versus its justification.
- **Criticism:** the atomism of impressions guarantees the conclusion.

#### 2024 · Q2(a) · 20 marks

**Question:** Is rejection of Locke’s notion of primary qualities instrumental in Berkeley’s leaning towards idealism? In this context, also discuss how subjective idealism is different from the absolute idealism proposed by Hegel.

##### Demand decoding

| Element | Requirement |
|---|---|
| Directive | Two limbs: an **instrumentality verdict**, then a **comparison** |
| Core issue | Which rejection actually does the work, and how two idealisms differ in kind |
| Scope boundary | Twenty marks: the quality distinction, parity and likeness, the abstraction ground, then matched axes for Hegel |
| Ideal thesis | Yes, but instrumentally through the likeness principle and anti-abstraction, not through relativity alone |
| Non-negotiables | Primary/secondary lists; §§8, 9–15; abstraction as the deeper ground; Berkeley subjective and theistic; Hegel objective and dialectical |

##### Independent model answer

Yes — but the answer must say **which** rejection is instrumental, since Berkeley uses two different weapons and only one of them is decisive.

Locke divides **primary** qualities — solidity, extension, figure, motion or rest, number — which are inseparable from body and whose ideas **resemble** them, from **secondary** qualities such as colour and warmth, which are **powers** to produce sensations and whose ideas resemble nothing.

Berkeley's first weapon is **parity** (*Principles* §§9–15): every relativity consideration Locke accepts against secondary qualities applies to primary ones as well — apparent size varies with distance, motion with frame of reference, shape with angle of view. This is suggestive but not decisive, since a realist may reply that varying appearance is exactly what a mind-independent shape would produce.

The decisive weapon is the **likeness principle** (§8): "an idea can be like nothing but an idea", and a resemblance to an unperceivable original could never be checked. That removes the last property matter was supposed to have in its own right. Behind both lies the deeper ground: the attack on **abstract general ideas**, since Locke's *material substance in general* is the abstract idea of being plus the relative notion of supporting. Once abstraction fails, matter is meaningless rather than unknown, and only spirits and ideas remain.

**Subjective versus absolute idealism.** For Berkeley, reality is **ideas in finite and divine minds**; sensible existence *consists in* being perceived, and an infinite spirit causes and coordinates the ideas of sense while sustaining what finite minds do not perceive. For Hegel, reality is the **self-development of Absolute Spirit**, in which objectivity is a moment within a dialectical process driven by determinate negation; the Absolute is not an external perceiver preserving private idea-collections, and its method is developmental rather than perceptual.

**Verdict.** ⚠️ The rejection of primary qualities is instrumental, but as a *removal* rather than a *proof*: it clears matter away, while the positive idealism comes from the anti-abstraction argument and from the argument to an active infinite spirit. And Berkeley's theistic subjective idealism is not an early Hegelianism — the two differ in **kind**, not in degree.

##### Why this earns marks

**Answer-writing focus — 2024 Q2(a):** "instrumental" asks for a causal-explanatory ruling, not a narration of two doctrines.

**Exam-length execution — 20 marks, about 340 to 400 words:** the distinction in three lines; parity in four; likeness and abstraction in six; the comparison in six; verdict in three.

- **Structure:** the distinction → parity → likeness → abstraction → matched-axis comparison → verdict.
- **Doctrine:** qualities; likeness; parity; abstraction; two idealisms.
- **Evidence:** *Essay* II.viii; *Principles* §§8, 9–15 and Intro §§6–25.
- **Distinction:** removal versus proof; subjective versus absolute idealism.
- **Criticism:** relativity alone does not entail mind-dependence.

#### 2025 · Q2(c) · 15 marks

**Question:** Examine Hume’s refutation of Causal relation and Kant’s response to it.

##### Demand decoding

| Element | Requirement |
|---|---|
| Directive | "Examine" both limbs — state each, then test whether Kant's answer succeeds |
| Core issue | Whether the Second Analogy answers Hume or presupposes what it must prove |
| Scope boundary | Fifteen marks: Hume compressed but complete, Kant precise, one adjudication |
| Ideal thesis | Kant accepts Hume's negative result and attacks the atomism that produced it |
| Non-negotiables | No impression of necessary connexion; custom; both definitions; Second Analogy; synthetic a priori; things in themselves |

##### Independent model answer

**Hume.** Observation of causal sequences delivers only temporal priority, spatial contiguity and constant conjunction; no impression of a power or tie is given, and repetition adds nothing to the objects. The internal route through volition fails equally. What repetition alters is the mind, which after constant conjunction is **determined** to pass from cause to effect — an **impression of reflection** from which the idea of necessity is copied. Hence two definitions of cause: regular succession of similars, and an object whose appearance conveys the thought to the other. The consequence is that inductive inference rests on **custom**, not reason.

**Kant.** He does not dispute the negative finding; he agrees that necessary connexion is not copied from a sensory impression. His answer is transcendental. In the **Second Analogy** the category of cause is an *a priori* rule required to distinguish an **objective** succession of events from the merely **subjective** succession of my perceptions. "Every event has a cause" is therefore **synthetic a priori** for possible experience: the understanding supplies necessary temporal ordering to phenomena, and objective empirical causation is rescued without any claim about causality among things in themselves.

**Examination.** The Humean challenge is that Kant has built the category into the definition of objective experience rather than proving it. Kant's reply is that without rule-governed synthesis, experience of an **event** would not be possible, so the presupposition is a condition of the fact to be explained.

**Verdict.** ⚠️ Kant is correctly aimed: Hume's conclusion follows from the **atomism of impressions**, and it is that atomism, not the inference, which Kant attacks. Whether a condition of possible experience amounts to knowledge of nature remains contested — which is why the answer should close by grading, not by declaring a winner.

##### Why this earns marks

**Answer-writing focus — 2025 Q2(c):** the Hume limb must be complete before Kant begins; ownership discipline is itself a mark.

**Exam-length execution — 15 marks, about 250 to 300 words:** Hume in nine lines; Kant in seven; the examination in four; verdict in two.

- **Structure:** Hume → Kant → the challenge and reply → graded verdict.
- **Doctrine:** copy principle; custom; two definitions; Second Analogy; synthetic a priori.
- **Evidence:** *Treatise* I.iii.14; *Enquiry* IV and VII; the ship-and-house contrast.
- **Distinction:** objective event-succession versus subjective succession of perceptions.
- **Criticism:** does Kant prove the category or presuppose it?

#### 2025 · Q4(c) · 15 marks

**Question:** Explain Berkeley’s doctrine of nominalism and his refutation of Abstract ideas.

##### Demand decoding

| Element | Requirement |
|---|---|
| Directive | "Explain" — exhibit the **derivation**, not the conclusion |
| Core issue | The doctrine refuted, the refutation, the replacement, and the metaphysical payload |
| Scope boundary | Fifteen marks: Locke's doctrine compressed, Berkeley's steps, the positive account, the consequence for matter |
| Ideal thesis | Nominalism is the foundation of immaterialism, not a corollary of it |
| Non-negotiables | III.iii and IV.vii.9; Intro §§6–25; §§12, 13, 19–20; matter as abstract general idea |

##### Independent model answer

**The doctrine refuted.** For Locke everything that exists is particular, yet most words are general; words become general by signifying **general ideas**, and ideas become general by **abstraction** — separation from circumstances of time, place and every concomitant idea (*Essay* III.iii.6–9). The resulting "general representative" is the **nominal essence** annexed to a sortal name. Locke himself concedes that the general idea of a triangle must be "neither oblique nor rectangle, neither equilateral, equicrural, nor scalenon; but all and none of these at once", and that it is "something imperfect, that cannot exist" (IV.vii.9).

**The refutation.** Berkeley concedes that we may separate what **could** exist apart — a hand from a body, a torso from a head. He denies that we may separate what **cannot**: extension without colour, motion without determinate speed, "man" without stature. He then quotes Locke's own triangle sentence verbatim at *Principles*, Introduction §13, and concludes that no such idea can be framed. Therefore there are no abstract general ideas.

**The replacement — nominalism proper.** An idea is general not by being abstract but "by being made to represent or stand for all other particular ideas of the same sort" (Intro §12). A determinate particular idea, **selectively attended to**, does duty for a class: the geometer draws this black inch-long line and proves a theorem about all lines. Berkeley adds that words need not stand for ideas at all (§§19–20).

**The payload.** Locke's *material substance in general* is precisely an abstract general idea — the abstract idea of being plus the relative notion of supporting. If abstraction is impossible, "matter" is not a mysterious unknown but a **meaningless expression**, so immaterialism rests on the nominalism.

**Verdict.** ⚠️ Decisive against an **imagistic** Locke; against a **determinable** reading the objection misses, and Berkeley still owes an account of what fixes the respect of representation.

##### Why this earns marks

**Answer-writing focus — 2025 Q4(c):** the refutation cannot be explained without the doctrine refuted. Give Locke first, compressed but exact.

**Exam-length execution — 15 marks, about 250 to 300 words:** Locke in six lines; the refutation in five; the replacement in five; the payload in three; verdict in two.

- **Structure:** doctrine refuted → refutation → positive replacement → metaphysical payload → verdict.
- **Doctrine:** abstraction; nominal essence; representative use; language beyond ideas.
- **Evidence:** IV.vii.9 quoted at *Principles* Intro §13; §12; §§19–20.
- **Distinction:** separating what could exist apart versus what could not.
- **Criticism:** the determinable reading of Locke, and the unfixed respect of representation.

### ORIGINAL MAINS PRACTICE WITH MODEL SOLUTIONS

> Six original questions in the UPSC idiom — two each at 10, 15 and 20 marks — with complete model solutions inside the same word bands. These are practice items, not predictions.

#### Original 1 · 10 marks

**Question:** Is Locke's description of substance as "something, I know not what" an admission of ignorance or an abandonment of the concept? Examine.

##### Independent model answer

It is an admission of ignorance, and the distinction is exact.

Locke observes that we meet clusters of qualities that regularly go together — yellow, heavy, malleable, soluble in aqua regia — and cannot conceive them as subsisting on their own. We therefore **suppose** a support. But no impression of that support is ever presented, so we have "a supposition of he knows not what support of such qualities" (*Essay* II.xxiii.2).

Two things are denied and one is affirmed. Denied: any **positive idea** of the substratum, and any knowledge of the **real essence** it embodies. Affirmed: that something is qualified, since qualities do not float.

The concept is therefore retained as an **explanatory posit** tied to the stability of kinds, not abandoned. Berkeley's counter is that "support" here is a dead metaphor, and that removing it leaves the abstract idea of being conjoined with a relative notion — an abstract general idea.

**Verdict.** ⚠️ Ignorance, not abandonment; but the posit's intelligibility depends on a doctrine of abstraction that Berkeley attacks, so Locke's humility is stable only while that doctrine holds.

##### Why this earns marks

**Answer-writing focus:** the question offers a false dichotomy, and the mark is for refusing it precisely.

**Exam-length execution — 10 marks, about 150 to 200 words:** the ruling in one line; the derivation in four; denied-and-affirmed in three; Berkeley in two; verdict in two.

- **Structure:** ruling → derivation → what is denied and affirmed → the counter → graded verdict.
- **Doctrine:** substratum; real essence; explanatory posit.
- **Evidence:** *Essay* II.xxiii.2.
- **Distinction:** no positive idea versus no existence.
- **Criticism:** the posit depends on abstraction.

#### Original 2 · 10 marks

**Question:** Berkeley holds that we have a "notion" of spirit though no idea of it. Is this distinction principled or merely convenient? Discuss.

##### Independent model answer

It is principled in its motive and vulnerable in its result.

The motive is internal to the system. Berkeley's ideas are **passive**; a spirit is **active**, perceiving and willing. An idea, being inert, could never picture an activity, so if activity is known at all it cannot be known by an idea. Hence spirits — my own, other finite minds, and God — are known by **notion** (*Principles* §§27, 89, 140). Notions also cover relations, which likewise are not pictured.

The vulnerability is equally clear. Berkeley's case against matter was that an unperceivable substratum has no content. An unperceivable spirit, known by a faculty introduced for the purpose, invites the same complaint, and critics call the asymmetry ad hoc.

The strongest defence is that the asymmetry is **forced** rather than chosen: the objection assumes that all knowledge is idea-shaped, which is precisely what Berkeley denies once activity enters the ontology. Hume then tests the defence by refusing the exemption and dissolving spiritual substance as well.

**Verdict.** ⚠️ Principled in derivation, unproven in adequacy — and Hume's refusal shows exactly how much weight it was carrying.

##### Why this earns marks

**Answer-writing focus:** "principled or convenient" demands a two-sided ruling, not a defence brief.

**Exam-length execution — 10 marks, about 150 to 200 words:** motive in five lines; vulnerability in three; defence in three; verdict in one.

- **Structure:** motive → vulnerability → defence → verdict.
- **Doctrine:** passivity of ideas; activity of spirits; notions.
- **Evidence:** *Principles* §§27, 89, 140.
- **Distinction:** forced asymmetry versus convenient exemption.
- **Criticism:** Hume removes the exemption and the self goes with it.

#### Original 3 · 15 marks

**Question:** "Berkeley destroys Locke's resemblance thesis but not his distinction between primary and secondary qualities." Critically examine.

##### Independent model answer

The statement is largely correct, and stating why requires separating two theses Locke runs together.

**Thesis A — the explanatory distinction.** Primary qualities are solidity, extension, figure, motion or rest and number: they are inseparable from body, since every fragment retains them, and they are the properties a corpuscular physics must attribute to any body. Secondary qualities are **powers**, grounded in primary-quality configurations, to produce sensations in us.

**Thesis B — the resemblance claim.** Our ideas of primary qualities **resemble** the qualities themselves; our ideas of secondary qualities resemble nothing in the object.

Berkeley attacks both. His **parity** argument (*Principles* §§9–15) observes that the relativity considerations Locke accepts against secondary qualities apply equally to primary ones. But relativity does not entail mind-dependence: that a coin looks elliptical from an angle is exactly what a genuinely circular coin should produce. Thesis A therefore survives, though it must be restated as a distinction between **measurable-structural** and **response-dependent** properties rather than as a claim about what is "really in" the object.

The **likeness principle** (§8) is another matter. If an idea can be like nothing but an idea, and the alleged originals are unperceivable, then the resemblance could never be checked and the claim is empty. Thesis B falls. The available rescue is **isomorphism**: structural correspondence requires no qualitative likeness, as a map corresponds to terrain without being terrain-coloured — but this concedes Berkeley's point about likeness and defends something weaker.

**Verdict.** ⚠️ Correct with one qualification: what survives is not Locke's distinction as he framed it, but its explanatory core. Locke's error was to make the division depend on a picture rather than on explanatory role — which is why physics retains the distinction while his version of it does not.

##### Why this earns marks

**Answer-writing focus:** a quotation-led "critically examine" requires you to grade the quotation, not merely to agree.

**Exam-length execution — 15 marks, about 250 to 300 words:** the two theses in seven lines; parity in five; likeness in five; verdict in four.

- **Structure:** separate the theses → parity against A → likeness against B → graded verdict.
- **Doctrine:** quality lists; powers; resemblance; isomorphism.
- **Evidence:** *Essay* II.viii; *Principles* §§8 and 9–15.
- **Distinction:** measurable-structural versus response-dependent properties.
- **Criticism:** the survivor is the explanatory core, not Locke's own formulation.

#### Original 4 · 15 marks

**Question:** Hume's Appendix confesses that he cannot explain what unites successive perceptions. Does this failure refute the bundle theory or diagnose a deeper problem? Discuss.

##### Independent model answer

It diagnoses a deeper problem, and reading it as a simple refutation misses what Hume has shown.

**The doctrine.** Applying the copy principle to the perceiver, Hume finds no constant and invariable impression of a self, only perceptions in perpetual flux, and concludes that the self is "nothing but a bundle or collection of different perceptions" (*Treatise* I.iv.6). The felt unity is a fiction produced by **resemblance** and **causation**, which let the imagination pass smoothly along the series and mistake an easy transition for an identical object.

**The confession.** In the Appendix Hume writes that all his hopes vanish when he comes to explain the principles that unite our successive perceptions. The admission is genuine, and the *Enquiry* silently drops the topic.

**What it shows.** Hume has denied two things at once: any **substance** in which perceptions inhere, and any **real connexion** between distinct existences. Together these remove every resource a uniting principle could have used. The failure is therefore not an accident of ingenuity but a consequence of the **atomism of impressions** — the doctrine that perceptions are separable atoms, so that no relation is given with its terms.

**The comparison that clinches it.** Exactly the same atomism generates the negative result about causation, where no relation between events can be perceived either. One premise, two failures.

**Verdict.** ⚠️ The bundle theory is not refuted by the confession; it is revealed as the last casualty of empiricist atomism. That Hume can find no principle of unity, and says so, indicts the premise rather than the self — which is precisely the diagnosis Kant makes when he supplies a formal condition of unity instead of an impression.

##### Why this earns marks

**Answer-writing focus:** the question offers two readings; choose one and show why the other is superficial.

**Exam-length execution — 15 marks, about 250 to 300 words:** doctrine in six lines; confession in three; diagnosis in six; comparison in two; verdict in three.

- **Structure:** doctrine → confession → diagnosis → parallel with causation → verdict.
- **Doctrine:** copy principle; bundle; fiction of identity; atomism.
- **Evidence:** *Treatise* I.iv.6 and the Appendix; the *Enquiry*'s silence.
- **Distinction:** refutation of a theory versus diagnosis of a premise.
- **Criticism:** one premise produces both the missing self and the missing necessity.

#### Original 5 · 20 marks

**Question:** "The movement from Locke through Berkeley to Hume is a logical development rather than a historical accident." Critically analyse.

##### Independent model answer

The claim is defensible but needs one important correction: the movement is logical only because each successor supplies an **additional premise** his predecessor could have refused.

**The shared rule.** All three accept that every simple cognitive content originates in experience — Locke's sensation and reflection, Hume's impressions — while retaining the mind's operations of comparing, combining and abstracting.

**Locke's exemption.** He applies the rule to ideas but exempts **matter**, holding that ideas of primary qualities resemble their causes and that a substratum supports co-occurring qualities, though we have "a supposition of he knows not what support" (*Essay* II.xxiii.2). Sensitive knowledge of external things is his weakest degree, so the exemption is asserted at the point of least support.

**Berkeley's step, and its premise.** He removes the exemption with the **likeness principle**: an idea can be like nothing but an idea, and a resemblance to an unperceivable original could never be checked (*Principles* §8). Beneath this lies the attack on abstraction, since "material substance in general" is the abstract idea of being plus the relative notion of supporting. The **added premise** is comparability — that a resemblance no perceiver could verify is empty. A determined Lockean could resist by shifting to structural correspondence, which requires no qualitative likeness.

**Hume's step, and its premise.** He applies the same test to what Berkeley retained: spirit and causal power. No impression of a substantial self is found, so the self becomes a bundle (*Treatise* I.iv.6); no impression of necessary connexion is found, so necessity is relocated to an impression of reflection. The **added premise** is the **atomism of impressions** — perceptions are separable, so no relation is given with its terms. Berkeley could have resisted by denying that activity must be idea-shaped, which is exactly what his doctrine of notions asserts.

**Verdict.** ⚠️ The sequence is logical in structure and contingent in premises. Each stage is the previous position made consistent, which is why it is not a historical accident; but each stage also needs a premise that was available to be denied, which is why it is not a proof. That the rule consistently applied ends in a scepticism nobody can live is best read as a diagnosis of the rule's incompleteness — the conclusion Kant draws when he attacks the atomism rather than the inference.

##### Why this earns marks

**Answer-writing focus:** a "logical development" claim is won by exhibiting the **mechanism** and lost by narrating three systems.

**Exam-length execution — 20 marks, about 340 to 400 words:** shared rule in three lines; Locke in five; Berkeley in six; Hume in six; verdict in five.

- **Structure:** shared rule → exemption → two removals with their added premises → verdict.
- **Doctrine:** resemblance; likeness; abstraction; bundle; necessary connexion.
- **Evidence:** II.xxiii.2; *Principles* §8 and Intro §§6–25; *Treatise* I.iv.6 and I.iii.14.
- **Distinction:** logical structure versus contingent premises.
- **Criticism:** each added premise was resistible, so the slide explains without proving.

#### Original 6 · 20 marks

**Question:** Compare the role assigned to God by Locke, Berkeley and Hume, and assess what each system loses if the divine premise is withdrawn.

##### Independent model answer

The comparison is best run on one axis: **what job does God perform in the system?** Answered that way, three very different dependencies appear.

**Locke — God as guarantor and conclusion.** God's existence is known by **demonstration**, not intuition and not sensitive knowledge: from the intuitive certainty of my own existence, together with the principle that nothing comes from nothing, Locke infers an eternal, most powerful and most knowing being. God also does defensive work: divine non-deception supports the reliability of sense and the trustworthiness of our faculties for "the conveniences of life".

*If withdrawn:* the demonstration is lost, and the defence of representative realism weakens — but the epistemology survives, since the three degrees of knowledge and the two fountains of ideas stand independently.

**Berkeley — God as cause and continuity.** Ideas are passive; the ideas of sense arrive involuntarily in settled order; only spirits are active; therefore an infinite spirit produces them (*Principles* §§30–33). God also secures the persistence of sensible things unperceived by finite minds, and the laws of nature become the settled grammar of a divine language, so regularities are **signs** rather than causes.

*If withdrawn:* the system collapses. Without an active infinite spirit there is no cause of the involuntary order, no continuity for unperceived objects, and no distinction between the real and the imaginary beyond the felt vivacity of one's own ideas. God is load-bearing here in a way he is not for Locke.

**Hume — God as casualty.** Every proof fails. The causal argument is cut at its premise, since there is no impression of necessary connexion licensing an inference to a transcendent cause. The design argument rests on a weak analogy, and even if granted proves at most a **finite** architect working on given matter; natural principles might explain order without intelligence; and the problem of evil blocks the inference to a perfectly good designer. Testimony can never make a miracle more probable than its own falsehood.

*If withdrawn:* nothing is lost, because nothing was resting on it.

**Verdict.** ⚠️ The dependency is inversely related to the empiricism's consistency. Locke uses God as a support, Berkeley requires him as a mechanism, Hume dispenses with him as unsupported. The comparison also exposes the deepest criticism of immaterialism: God performs exactly the two functions Locke assigned to matter — causing our ideas and sustaining the unperceived — so Berkeley's economy is a substitution of posits rather than an elimination of them.

##### Why this earns marks

**Answer-writing focus:** announce the axis — the **job God performs** — in the first line, and the comparison writes itself.

**Exam-length execution — 20 marks, about 340 to 400 words:** axis in one line; each thinker in a paragraph with an explicit withdrawal test; verdict in four.

- **Structure:** axis → three systems each with a counterfactual test → verdict.
- **Doctrine:** demonstrative proof; divine non-deception; passivity and continuity; the three failed proofs.
- **Evidence:** *Essay* IV; *Principles* §§30–33; *Dialogues concerning Natural Religion*; *Enquiry* X.
- **Distinction:** God as support, as mechanism, and as casualty.
- **Criticism:** in Berkeley, God does matter's old job — which is the real question about the system.

## OPTIONAL ADVANCED DEPTH — NOT REQUIRED FOR A CORE ANSWER

### Prerequisite check before using this block

Nothing below is needed to write a complete answer to any of the twelve routed questions. Use it only after the thirteen Basic sessions, the thirty-two diagnostics and the solved practice are secure, and then only as a single evaluative rider inside a fifteen- or twenty-mark answer. Every item here is a **live scholarly dispute** and is marked ❓ accordingly; presenting any of these readings as settled doctrine would be a mistake in the opposite direction from omitting them.

### A1. Locke on real essence and corpuscular explanation ❓

- **The issue:** is Locke a humble realist about hidden structures, or does his talk of substratum and real essence amount to a sceptical placeholder?
- **Competing readings:** a realist or **corpuscular** reading, on which real essences are genuine internal constitutions awaiting discovery; an **anti-essentialist** reading that stresses nominal essence and epistemic modesty; and a middle reading on which real essence exists but rarely functions as usable knowledge.
- **The further objection and the counter-reply:** *Objection* — "something I know not what" looks empty. *Counter-reply* — Locke can say it is an explanatory posit tied to stable powers and kinds, not a positively known object of intuition.
- **Safe use ⚠️:** one brief refinement inside a Locke substance answer, or when explaining why Berkeley finds Locke unstable.
- **Do not over-study:** the debates on corpuscular chemistry are not marks-essential unless a question explicitly asks about real essence.

### A2. Berkeley's Master Argument after the textbook summary ❓

- **The issue:** does Berkeley really prove that an unperceived object is inconceivable, or only that one cannot *at the same time* conceive an object while conceiving it as unconceived?
- **Competing readings:** a strong **conceivability** argument; an **anti-abstraction polemic** in which the challenge is a corollary of the Introduction; and a **dialectical** reading that targets Locke's representational assumptions more than common realism.
- **The further objection and the counter-reply:** *Objection* — I can surely think of a tree standing unperceived in a forest. *Counter-reply* — Berkeley answers that the tree is still conceived *by you* in the very act of the thought; his target is "conceived-as-unconceived", not "not currently seen".
- **Safe use ⚠️:** only after *esse est percipi* and the attack on abstract ideas are already in place.
- **Do not over-study:** do not turn the answer into a survey of the analytic literature on conceivability.

### A3. Hume the destructive sceptic versus Hume the naturalist ❓

- **The issue:** does Hume merely dissolve reason into habit, or does he reconstruct philosophy on the basis of ordinary human practice?
- **Competing readings:** the **radical sceptic** reading; the **mitigated or naturalist** reading; and a combined reading on which sceptical critique clears the way for modest common life and science.
- **The further objection and the counter-reply:** *Objection* — appealing to custom looks like surrender, not philosophy. *Counter-reply* — Hume's point is that human cognition inescapably runs on habit, and philosophy should explain that limit rather than pretend to escape it by rationalist fiat.
- **Safe use ⚠️:** one closing evaluative paragraph in an answer on causation, the self or scepticism. It must never replace the copy principle, the bundle theory or the habit analysis.
- **Do not over-study:** Hume's political, historical and economic writings are outside this owner.

### A4. The reflexive status of Hume's fork ❓

- **The issue:** into which of its own two boxes does the fork itself fall? If it is neither a relation of ideas nor a matter of fact, it appears to license its own consignment to the flames.
- **Competing readings:** the **self-refutation** charge; a **meta-level** reading on which the fork is a clarification of the meanings of terms rather than a first-order claim; and a **methodological** reading on which it is a proposal for enquiry, assessed by fruitfulness rather than by truth.
- **The residual ❓:** each rescue makes the fork less like a discovery and more like a stipulation, which weakens every conclusion that leans on its exhaustiveness — most obviously the induction dilemma.
- **Safe use ⚠️:** one clause in a scepticism or induction answer, after the dilemma has been set out properly.

### A5. The modal status of the copy principle after the missing shade ❓

- **The issue:** is the copy principle an empirical generalisation, an analytic truth about what an idea is, or a methodological rule for detecting empty terms?
- **Competing readings:** an **empirical** reading, which is what Hume's own concession suggests; a **constitutive** reading, on which nothing would count as an idea without an antecedent impression; and a **regulative** reading, on which the principle is a test rather than a law.
- **The further objection and the counter-reply:** *Objection* — if it is empirical, one admitted exception invites others, and the demolitions of substance, self and necessary connexion lose their force. *Counter-reply* — interpolation between impressions actually had preserves the dependence on experiential materials, so the exception is parasitic on the rule rather than a rival to it.
- **Safe use ⚠️:** a single sentence when an answer needs to grade rather than merely report Hume's negative results.

### A6. Is Berkeley's God an advance on Locke's matter? ❓

- **The issue:** immaterialism removes an unknowable substratum and installs an unperceivable spirit that performs the same two functions — causing our ideas and sustaining the unperceived.
- **Competing readings:** an **economy** reading, on which spirits are already required for perception so no new category is added; a **substitution** reading, on which the change is one of vocabulary; and a **theological** reading, on which the point is precisely to make the world's order personal rather than mechanical.
- **The further objection and the counter-reply:** *Objection* — the notion doctrine is an exemption introduced to protect the favoured posit. *Counter-reply* — the exemption is forced, since a passive idea could never picture an activity, and Berkeley applies the doctrine to finite spirits and relations as well as to God.
- **Safe use ⚠️:** as the residual column of the criticism ledger in a twenty-mark answer on immaterialism.

### Conceptual links — not current-affairs items

Static early-modern philosophy does not require a manufactured contemporary anchor, and none has been forced here. The genuine conceptual links, usable as a closing clause when a question invites breadth, are: the fork running forward into the verification principle of logical positivism; the problem of induction remaining unsolved in the philosophy of science; the bundle self standing beside the Buddhist doctrine of no-self as a **parallel** rather than an influence; and the primary–secondary distinction surviving in science as a contrast between measurable-structural and response-dependent properties. Each belongs to a **later or different owner** and should be named in a clause, not developed.

### Source trail for this block

Locke, *An Essay Concerning Human Understanding*, Books II–IV; Berkeley, *A Treatise concerning the Principles of Human Knowledge* and *Three Dialogues between Hylas and Philonous*; Hume, *A Treatise of Human Nature* Book I with the Appendix, *An Enquiry concerning Human Understanding* Sections II, IV, V, VII, X and XII, and the *Dialogues concerning Natural Religion*. The interpretive positions summarised above follow the repository's advanced Western Philosophy dossier, which names Michael Ayers, Kenneth Winkler, George Pappas, Norman Kemp Smith, Barry Stroud and Don Garrett as the standard modern discussants, together with the scope objection to the Master Argument associated with Russell and refined by Prior and Gallois.

## CONSOLIDATED REGISTER NOTES

> Compressed revision notes for the complete topic, arranged by examinable structure rather than by a fixed template. Read these last, and read them fast. Parts A to L cover every Basic session; the optional advanced material is deliberately excluded, because none of it is required for a core answer.

### A. The empiricist operating rule and the four printed test cases

- **Origin, not warrant.** Every **simple** cognitive content traces to experience: Locke's **sensation** (outer) and **reflection** (inner), Hume's **impressions**. Mental operations — comparing, combining, abstracting — are never denied.
- The syllabus prints four test cases: **theory of knowledge · substance and qualities · self and God · scepticism**. Show all four in any general answer.
- The added premise at each step: Locke assumes **resemblance**; Berkeley adds **comparability** ("an idea can be like nothing but an idea"); Hume adds the **atomism of impressions**.
- Landing points: **representative realism → subjective idealism → mitigated scepticism**.
- Memory chain: **R → I → S**; deletions **M-S-N** (Berkeley deletes Matter; Hume deletes the Substantial self and objective Necessity); verdicts on scepticism **H-A-M** (Humble, Anti-sceptical, Mitigated).
- ❌ Not anti-reason; ❌ not the five senses only; ❌ not three biographies.

### B. Locke — anti-innatism and the sources of ideas

```text
three grounds -> white paper -> sensation + reflection -> simple ideas ->
combine/compare/abstract -> complex ideas (modes | substances | relations)
```

- Three grounds: **universal assent fails** · **no unconscious ideas** · **a genetic account is available**.
- The decisive ground is the second, because "to be in the mind is to be perceived by the mind" is a **stipulation** that blocks the dispositional retreat.
- *Essay* II.i.2: the mind as "**white paper, void of all characters**". ⚠️ *Tabula rasa* is the tradition's label, **not** Locke's phrase.
- Simple ideas passive; complex ideas actively made as **modes** (triangle, gratitude, number), **substances** (gold, man) and **relations** (cause, identity).
- ⚠️ Verdict: naive innatism destroyed; **dispositional** innatism survives. Leibniz's rejoinder — nothing in the intellect that was not in the senses, **except the intellect itself** — names the survivor, and Kant vindicates its structural form.

### C. Locke — knowledge, the three degrees, probability and the veil

- **Knowledge = the perception of the agreement or disagreement of ideas.**
- Degrees: **intuitive** ("white is not black") → **demonstrative** (mathematics; the existence of God) → **sensitive** (particular existing things). The external world sits on the **weakest** rung.
- Below knowledge lies **probability and judgement**: testimony, analogy, natural history, everyday expectation.
- **Representative realism:** body → sense organ → **idea**, which is the only inspectable item; primary-quality ideas are said to **resemble**.
- Veil objection: the comparison can never be made. Locke's four defences — **involuntariness**, **inter-sensory agreement**, **pleasure and pain**, **divine non-deception**.
- ✅ Survives: **causal and structural** representation. ❌ Exposed: **qualitative resemblance**.
- Locke is epistemically humble, not sceptical: the candle burns bright enough for the conduct of life.

### D. Locke — abstraction, nominal essence and real essence

- Argument: all existents are particular (III.iii.1) → separate names impossible (III.iii.2–4) → general terms needed → words general by signifying **general ideas** (III.iii.6) → ideas made general by **separation from circumstances of time, place and concomitants** (III.iii.6–9) → a **general representative** → the **nominal essence** annexed to the name (III.iii.15) → universals are "the inventions and creatures of the understanding" (III.iii.11).
- **Nominal essence** = the abstract idea attached to the general name (gold: yellow, heavy, malleable, soluble in aqua regia). **Real essence** = the internal constitution producing observable properties; generally unknown.
- Locke is a **conceptualist**, not a Platonic realist and not a bare nominalist.
- The concession (IV.vii.9): the general triangle is "neither oblique nor rectangle, neither equilateral, equicrural, nor scalenon; but all and none of these at once", and is "**something imperfect, that cannot exist**".
- Best reply: **partial, not contradictory** — a determinable with angles left undetermined. Price: the abstract idea stops being imagistic.
- Four presuppositions: ideational meaning · imagistic ideas · possible subtraction · one idea per general word.

### E. Locke — qualities and the substratum

| | Primary | Secondary |
|---|---|---|
| List | solidity, extension, figure, motion or rest, number | colour, sound, taste, smell, warmth or cold |
| Status | really in the object; inseparable from body | **powers** to produce sensations in us |
| Ideas | **resemble** the qualities | resemble nothing in the object |
| Test | every fragment retains them | vary with the perceiver's organs |

- Two separable theses: **A** the explanatory division; **B** the resemblance picture.
- Berkeley's **parity** (§§9–15) attacks A — answered, since relativity is what a real shape would produce. Berkeley's **likeness** (§8) attacks B — this lands.
- Survivor: **measurable-structural versus response-dependent** properties, defended by **isomorphism**, not by likeness.
- Substratum (II.xxiii.2): "a supposition of he knows not what support of such qualities" — **nature** unknown, **existence** not denied.
- Berkeley's next move: an unknowable support is an **abstract general idea** (being + supporting), so matter becomes **meaningless**, not mysterious.

### F. Locke — personal identity and God

- Three identities: **substance** · **man** (the continuing living organism) · **person**.
- Person (II.xxvii.9, chapter **added in the second edition of 1694**): "a thinking intelligent being, that has reason and reflection, and can consider itself as itself, the same thinking thing, in different times and places".
- Criterion: the extension of **consciousness** by **appropriation**; memory is vehicle and evidence, not the definition. "Person" is a **forensic** term.
- **Prince and cobbler:** person follows consciousness, man follows the organism.
- Objections: **Butler and Reid** — circularity, since memory presupposes identity; **Reid's Brave Officer** — transitivity fails; **gaps** — sleep and amnesia.
- Repairs: **overlapping chains** of consciousness; continuous **capacity** rather than constant recollection.
- ⚠️ Verdict: forensically indispensable, metaphysically incomplete.
- **God:** known by **demonstration** — nothing comes from nothing; I exist; therefore an eternal, most powerful and most knowing being. ⚠️ The exposed premise is the causal principle Hume later refuses to certify. ❌ No innate idea of God.

### G. Berkeley — nominalism and the anti-abstraction base

- **Allowed:** separating parts that **could** exist apart (a hand, a headless torso). **Denied:** separating what **cannot** (extension without colour; motion without determinate speed; "man" without stature).
- Locke's triangle is quoted **verbatim** at *Principles*, Introduction §13, and declared self-contradictory.
- Replacement (§12): an idea is general "**by being made to represent or stand for all other particular ideas of the same sort**" — a determinate particular, **selectively attended to**. The geometer draws this inch-long line and proves a theorem about all lines.
- §§19–20: words do not always stand for ideas; language also raises passion, prompts action and disposes the mind. ⚠️ A genuine anticipation of the later claim that meaning is use.
- **Payload:** matter = the abstract idea of *being* + the relative notion of *supporting*; so **immaterialism rests on the nominalism, not the reverse**.
- Limits: the attack works against **imagistic** Locke; and Berkeley owes an account of what fixes the **respect** of representation.

### H. Berkeley — the five arguments and *esse est percipi*

```text
semantic (Sec.1-4) -> likeness (Sec.8) -> parity (Sec.9-15) ->
master (Sec.22-23) -> continuity (Sec.6, 30-33, 48)
```

1. **Semantic:** objects of knowledge are ideas; "sensible things" means what is immediately perceived; an apple or a tree is a **collection of ideas**; ideas cannot exist unperceived; therefore *esse est percipi* (§3), and "absolute existence without relation to their being perceived" is unintelligible. ⚠️ Step three is a **definition**, not a discovery.
2. **Likeness (§8):** an idea can be like nothing but an idea; an unverifiable resemblance is empty. **Objection:** isomorphism needs no qualitative likeness.
3. **Parity (§§9–15):** Locke's own relativity considerations apply to primary qualities too. **Objection:** relativity does not entail mind-dependence.
4. **Master (§§22–23):** you cannot conceive a body unconceived. **Objection (Russell; refined by Prior and Gallois):** a scope or use–mention confusion. **Berkeley's reply:** question-begging only if immaterialism is already false.
5. **Continuity (§§6, 30–33, 48):** ideas are passive, sense-ideas arrive involuntarily in order, only spirits act, therefore God — and unperceived things persist in the infinite Mind.
- Reality versus imagination: **involuntary, vivid, coherent, law-governed**. Illusion is an error of **judgment**, not of existence.
- ❌ Never "Berkeley denies the external world". ⚠️ *Esse est percipi aut percipere* is a **later expansion**, not his Latin.

### I. Berkeley — spirits, notions, God and the two required comparisons

- Ontology: **spirits** (active substances that perceive and will) + **ideas** (passive). Nothing else.
- Spirits are known by **notion** (§§27, 89, 140), not by idea, because a passive idea could never picture an activity. Critics call it ad hoc; Berkeley holds the asymmetry is forced.
- **God:** derived from the passivity of ideas plus the involuntary order of sense; secures **continuity**; the laws of nature are the settled grammar of a divine language — regularities are **signs**, not causes.
- Other finite minds: inferred by **analogy** from purposive signs. Solipsism blocked, but knowledge of other spirits is less immediate.
- Common sense: Berkeley claims to save it, and blames the **materialist** for inventing the unknowable world. ⚠️ Strong about objects, weaker about causes. The Johnson stone anecdote is **reception**, not refutation.
- **Moore:** the act–object distinction — awareness of blue is not blue; transparency supports **direct realism**. **Russell:** ordinary objects are **logical constructions** from sense-data, perspectives and relations. Shared ground: no reduction of object to act. **The difference:** Moore *defends* the ordinary object, Russell *replaces* it.
- **Hegel contrast:** Berkeley — reality is ideas in finite and divine minds, and God is an infinite **perceiver**; Hegel — reality is the self-development of **Absolute Spirit**, objectivity a moment within dialectic. Different in **kind**, not degree.

### J. Hume — impressions and ideas, the fork, substance and the belief in body

- Perceptions = **impressions** (vivid) + **ideas** (faint copies); the criterion is **force and vivacity**.
- **Copy principle:** every simple idea derives from a simple impression. ❓ Conceded exception: the **missing shade of blue** (*Treatise* I.i.1; *Enquiry* II), which shows the principle is an empirical generalisation. **Reply:** interpolation between impressions actually had.
- **Association:** resemblance · contiguity in time or place · cause and effect. Psychological, not logical — and it powers both the fiction of the self and causal belief.
- **The fork:** relations of ideas (a priori, necessary, denial contradictory) versus matters of fact (a posteriori, contingent, contrary conceivable). Neither → "**commit it then to the flames**" (*Enquiry* XII), aimed at divinity or school metaphysics. Direct ancestor of the verification principle.
- **Substance:** no impression of a bearer → a **fiction of the imagination**; dissolves **material and spiritual** substance alike, which is where Hume passes Berkeley.
- **Belief in body** (*Treatise* I.iv.2): sense gives only interrupted perceptions; **constancy** and **coherence** lead the imagination to feign continued and distinct objects; the philosophers' **double-existence** theory is unverifiable because all comparison stays within perceptions. ⚠️ Reason cannot justify the belief; **nature** compels it.

### K. Hume — the self, God and mitigated scepticism

- No constant and invariable impression of a self; only perceptions in **perpetual flux**.
- *Treatise* I.iv.6: the self is "**a bundle or collection of different perceptions, which succeed each other with an inconceivable rapidity**".
- The felt unity is a **fiction** produced by **resemblance** and **causation**: an easy transition mistaken for an identical object.
- **Appendix:** "all my hopes vanish when I come to explain the principles that unite our successive perceptions." ⚠️ Having denied both substance and real connexion, no resource remains — the failure indicts the **atomism**. The *Enquiry* silently drops the topic.
- **Kant:** accepts that no soul-substance is found; the "**I think**" must be able to accompany all representations; the **transcendental unity of apperception** is a **formal condition**, not an entity. Humean rejoinder: a condition of description, not a discovered subject.
- **God — three fronts, all lost.** Causal: no impression of a first cause or of necessary connexion. Design (*Dialogues*): weak analogy; at most a **finite** architect; natural order without intelligence; the problem of evil. Miracles (*Enquiry* X): testimony is always less probable than its own falsehood.
- **Scepticism:** **excessive or Pyrrhonian** suspension is unliveable; **mitigated** scepticism limits enquiry to what experience can answer, proportions belief to evidence and relies on natural belief. ❓ Stable only if natural beliefs are exempt from the demand for justification.

### L. Hume on causation and induction; exam craft, PYQ routes and the trap sheet

```text
observe: priority + contiguity + constant conjunction
not observed: power or tie
internal search: no impression of how the will moves the limb
found: the MIND'S DETERMINATION = impression of reflection
-> two definitions of cause -> custom -> the induction dilemma
```

- **Two definitions:** (i) an object followed by another where all similar objects are so followed — objective, regularity-based; (ii) an object whose appearance **conveys the thought** to the other — psychological. ⚠️ Never reconciled; the origin of the regularity-versus-projectivist debate.
- **Induction (*Enquiry* IV):** the Uniformity Principle is neither **demonstrable** (its denial is conceivable) nor **probable** (every experiential argument presupposes it), so inference rests on **custom**, "the great guide of human life" (*Enquiry* V). Every serious reply attacks **step 3**, the demonstrative-or-probable division — Kant with the synthetic a priori, Popper by denying that science needs induction, Strawson by dissolving the demand.
- **Kant's Second Analogy:** the category of cause is an *a priori* rule needed to distinguish **objective** event-succession from the **subjective** succession of perceptions; "every event has a cause" is synthetic a priori for possible experience, with no claim about things in themselves.

**PYQ routes — all twelve, in one place**

| Year | Part | Marks | Route |
|---|---|---:|---|
| 2018 | Q1(a) | 10 | The fork: relation of ideas versus matter of fact; conceivability test |
| 2018 | Q3(a) | 20 | Five arguments for immaterialism, then Moore's act–object realism versus Russell's logical construction, then the contrast |
| 2019 | Q4(b) | 15 | Not in objects, yes in the mind: the impression of reflection; both definitions |
| 2020 | Q2(a) | 20 | Bundle self, fiction of unity, Appendix; then Kant's transcendental unity of apperception |
| 2021 | Q1(c) | 10 | "Immediately perceived": the semantic argument and the removal of the veil |
| 2021 | Q1(d) | 10 | Person/man/substance; consciousness and appropriation; Butler–Reid; verdict |
| 2021 | Q1(e) | 10 | Constant conjunction is necessary, not sufficient; the felt determination completes it |
| 2022 | Q4(c) | 15 | Three grounds; white paper; sensation and reflection; the three degrees |
| 2023 | Q2(a) | 20 | Full causal analysis; association and custom; three objections; ruling |
| 2024 | Q2(a) | 20 | Parity and likeness, abstraction as the deeper ground; Berkeley versus Hegel on matched axes |
| 2025 | Q2(c) | 15 | Hume complete, then the Second Analogy, then adjudication |
| 2025 | Q4(c) | 15 | Locke's abstraction, Berkeley's refutation, the §12 replacement, the payload |

**Directive decoder in one line each.** Explain = give the **derivation**. Examine = state **and** test. Critically analyse = exposition **plus** verdict. Discuss = roughly 60:40 exposition to assessment. "Is there any element…?" = a **yes-and-no with a distinction**. "Is A instrumental in B?" = a causal-explanatory ruling.

**Provenance rules that protect marks.** *Tabula rasa* is a later label; Locke writes "white paper, void of all characters" (II.i.2). *Esse est percipi* is Berkeley's own Latin at §3; *aut percipere* is a commentators' expansion. II.xxvii was added in **1694**. The *Essay* appeared in **1689** with **1690** on the title page. The *Treatise* and the *Enquiry* must not be merged; the *Enquiry* drops personal identity, and it appeared in 1748 as *Philosophical Essays* before being renamed in 1758. "Reason is the slave of the passions" is *Treatise* II.iii.3 and belongs to ethics, not epistemology. Samuel Johnson's stone is reception, not argument.

**Trap sheet — ten lines**

1. "Berkeley denies the external world" — he denies **matter as an unperceiving substratum**.
2. "Hume denies causation" — he denies **objective necessary connexion**, not causal inference.
3. "Secondary qualities are illusions" — they are **powers**.
4. "Locke denies substance" — he denies **knowledge of its nature**.
5. "*Tabula rasa*" quoted as Locke's words — it is the tradition's label.
6. "Hume says the self does not exist" — no **simple identical substance**; the perceptions are real.
7. "Kant proves the soul" — he supplies a **formal condition** of unified experience.
8. Berkeley called a sceptic — he is an **anti-sceptic** who blames the materialist.
9. Berkeley called an early Hegel — subjective versus absolute idealism differ in **kind**.
10. Quine's "Two Dogmas of Empiricism" imported here — it belongs to Topic 11.

**Memory chain — say it in fifteen seconds.** *One rule about the origin of content, applied three times. Locke keeps matter he cannot inspect; Berkeley deletes matter and keeps spirits and God; Hume deletes substance, the simple self and objective necessity, and keeps perceptions, custom and natural belief. Necessity moves from the object to the observer. Kant enters only where a question routes him — apperception for the self, the Second Analogy for causation.*

```ascii-master
INNATIST CLAIM UNDER ATTACK (Essay, Book I): certain ideas and principles
- God, identity, non-contradiction, moral rules - are stamped on the mind

        |

        |    a thought the mind does not think is self-contradictory,
        |    because for Locke "to be IN the mind" = "to be PERCEIVED by
        |    the mind"
        |    TARGET: the retreat to ideas present but unnoticed
        |    STATUS: a STIPULATION about the meaning of "idea", not an
        |    observation - and this is what closes the escape route
        |

             every idea CLAIMED innate can be traced to sensation or
             reflection; if experience suffices, innateness is idle
             STATUS: empirical; inconclusive against a capacity claim
        |
        v
DESCARTES' DISPOSITIONAL REPLY: innate ideas were never claimed to be
consciously present from birth; they are CAPACITIES of the intellect,
elicited on an appropriate occasion, as a family disease is innate
before it appears.
        |
        v
ADJUDICATION
   AGAINST NAIVE INNATISM (consciously possessed inborn propositions):
      Locke wins outright.
   AGAINST DISPOSITIONAL INNATISM: ground 1 misses, ground 3 is
      inconclusive, and everything turns on ground 2 - a stipulation.
   THE SURVIVOR, NAMED: Leibniz - "nothing is in the intellect that was
      not first in the senses, EXCEPT THE INTELLECT ITSELF". Innatism
      survives as a thesis about STRUCTURE, the form Kant vindicates.
```

```ascii-master
PAYLOAD THAT MOST SCRIPTS MISS: "substance" appears HERE as a COMPLEX
IDEA. That is already the admission Session 5 exploits - we have a
CLUSTER plus a SUPPOSITION, never an inspected substratum.
WHY REFLECTION MATTERS: it lets Locke be an empiricist about logic,
mathematics and the self without innate content - and it is the channel
Hume later exploits when he locates the impression of necessary
connexion INTERNALLY.

passive; complex ideas are the WORKMANSHIP OF THE UNDERSTANDING.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 12/48: Locke: abstraction - the eight numbered steps and the general representative

```ascii-master
==============================================================================
THE PROBLEM: everything that EXISTS is particular; almost every WORD is
general. How can a mind furnished only with particular ideas use general
words?
        |
        v
THE ARGUMENT, NUMBERED                        Essay III.iii and IV.vii.9
 1 Everything that exists is PARTICULAR                     III.iii.1
 2 A separate name for every particular is impossible -
   memory could not hold them and communication would fail  III.iii.2-4
 3 THEREFORE language requires GENERAL TERMS
 4 Words are general ONLY by being signs of GENERAL IDEAS   III.iii.6
   (meaning is always mediated by an idea in the mind)
 5 Ideas become general when the mind SEPARATES them from
   circumstances of time, place and every concomitant idea  III.iii.6-9
 6 What remains serves as a "GENERAL REPRESENTATIVE" of all
   particulars of the same sort
 7 The abstract idea annexed to a sortal name IS the
   NOMINAL ESSENCE by which we classify                     III.iii.15
   -> classification is the WORKMANSHIP OF THE UNDERSTANDING,
      not a reading-off of real essences
 8 "General and universal belong not to the real existence of
   things; they are the inventions and creatures of the
   understanding, made by it for its own use, and concern
   only signs"                                              III.iii.11
        |
        v
POSITION NAMED: LOCKE IS A CONCEPTUALIST - neither a Platonic realist
about universals nor a bare nominalist. Naming the position is a
one-word gain in precision most scripts never make.
        |
        v
WHY THE DOCTRINE IS NOT OPTIONAL: without it Locke has no account of
classification, of definition, or of the nominal essences on which the
whole of Book III depends. Berkeley understood this exactly, which is
why he attacks ABSTRACTION FIRST, in the INTRODUCTION, before saying a
word about matter.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 17/48: Locke: the substratum, stated exactly, defended fairly, and attacked

```ascii-master
==============================================================================
WE MEET CLUSTERS THAT REGULARLY GO TOGETHER
   yellow + heavy + malleable + soluble in aqua regia = "gold"
        |
   WE CANNOT CONCEIVE QUALITIES AS SUBSISTING BY THEMSELVES
        |
        v
SO WE SUPPOSE A SUBSTRATUM THAT SUPPORTS THEM
   Essay II.xxiii.2 - the exact words:
   "a supposition of he knows not what support of such qualities"

        +--> WHAT IS AFFIRMED: that something is qualified - qualities

        |
        v
BERKELEY'S REPLY - AND WHY IT IS DECISIVE FOR HIS SYSTEM
   "Support" here is a DEAD METAPHOR borrowed from pillars and floors.
   Strip the metaphor and what remains is the ABSTRACT IDEA OF BEING
   plus the RELATIVE NOTION OF SUPPORTING - which is precisely an
   ABSTRACT GENERAL IDEA.
   THEREFORE, if abstraction is impossible, "matter" is not a mysterious
   UNKNOWN but a MEANINGLESS EXPRESSION.
   THEREFORE Berkeley's IMMATERIALISM RESTS ON HIS NOMINALISM, not the
   reverse.
        |
        v
FIX THE THREE POSITIONS BY THEIR VERBS - THEY ARE EASY TO SWAP
   LOCKE    SUPPOSES a support whose nature is unknown
   BERKELEY DENIES THE SENSE of the expression "material substance"
   HUME     DISSOLVES substance into a stable BUNDLE of qualities, and
            does so for SPIRITUAL substance as well
TRAP: "Locke denies substance" is false. He denies KNOWLEDGE OF ITS
NATURE and affirms its existence.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 20/48: Locke: the objections to personal identity, the repairs and the verdict

```ascii-master
==============================================================================
OBJECTION 1 - CIRCULARITY                     SOURCE: Butler, Reid
+-------------------------------------------------------------------+
| I can remember only MY OWN experiences. So "genuine memory" already|
| presupposes identity. If identity is DEFINED by memory, the        |
| definition is circular.                                            |
+-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   REPLY: distinguish the CONSTITUTIVE criterion (consciousness) from
     the EVIDENTIAL criterion (memory).
   RESIDUAL: "consciousness of X AS MINE" may smuggle the same
     ownership back in. The charge remains genuinely open.
        |
OBJECTION 2 - TRANSITIVITY FAILS: REID'S BRAVE OFFICER
+-------------------------------------------------------------------+
|  BOY (flogged at school)   <--remembers--   YOUNG OFFICER          |
|  YOUNG OFFICER             <--remembers--   OLD GENERAL            |
|  BOY                       <--NOT remembered by--  OLD GENERAL     |
|  So: boy = officer, officer = general, but boy =/= general.        |
|  Identity is TRANSITIVE. Locke's criterion, read as bare episodic  |
|  memory, is not.                                                   |
+-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   REPAIR: OVERLAPPING CHAINS - identity is the ANCESTRAL of direct
     appropriation.
   RESIDUAL: transitivity is restored, but the theory is no longer the
     simple memory criterion the textbooks attribute to Locke.
        |
OBJECTION 3 - GAPS: dreamless sleep, anaesthesia, amnesia
   REPLY: continuous CAPACITY for appropriation, not constant
     recollection.
   RESIDUAL: capacity-talk strains the account, and Locke concedes that
     whether the same substance persists through a gap is unknowable.
        |
        v
EXECUTABLE VERDICT: best defended as a REPAIRED PSYCHOLOGICAL-CONTINUITY
account. On that reading it survives Reid; the circularity charge stays
open. FORENSICALLY INDISPENSABLE, METAPHYSICALLY INCOMPLETE.
   Saying exactly this is what the directive "examine" requires.
```

```ascii-master
   NOT by SENSITIVE knowledge (reserved for particular present things)

THE CHAIN

   2 Something cannot come from nothing
   3 Therefore there has existed FROM ETERNITY a most powerful and most

empiricist framework. God is reached ABOVE the external world and BELOW

THE EXPOSED PREMISE - THE CLEANEST LINK IN THE WHOLE ITEM
   Step 2 is the CAUSAL PRINCIPLE. Hume will refuse to certify it,
   because there is NO IMPRESSION of causal necessity. Locke's proof of
   God therefore fails at exactly the point where Hume's analysis of
   causation bites.
   -> Use this link in ANY comparative answer on God in this item.

WHAT LOCKE DOES NOT SAY
   There is NO INNATE IDEA OF GOD. That was one of the candidates Book I
   explicitly dismissed - so an answer that gives Locke an innate idea
   of God contradicts the foundation of his system.
COMPARISON PREVIEW: Locke uses God as a SUPPORT (guarantor of our
faculties); Berkeley REQUIRES him as a MECHANISM (cause and continuity);
Hume treats him as a CASUALTY (no proof survives).
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 22/48: Berkeley: the demolition of abstraction, the replacement and the payload

```ascii-master
==============================================================================
BERKELEY'S DEMOLITION, NUMBERED       Principles, Introduction Sec.6-25
 1 I CONCEDE I can imagine particular things I have perceived, and
   compound and divide such ideas - a hand without a body, a headless
   torso. This is SEPARATION OF PARTS THAT COULD EXIST APART.
 2 But ABSTRACTION PROPER requires separating what CANNOT exist apart:
   extension without any colour; motion without a determinate speed;
   "man" without any determinate stature or complexion.
 3 Locke's own triangle - neither equilateral nor scalene, yet ALL AND
   NONE at once - is SELF-CONTRADICTORY; no such idea can be framed.
   (Berkeley quotes the passage VERBATIM at Intro Sec.13.)
 4 THEREFORE there are no abstract general ideas.
 5 POSITIVE REPLACEMENT: an idea is general not by being abstract but
   "BY BEING MADE TO REPRESENT OR STAND FOR ALL OTHER PARTICULAR IDEAS
   OF THE SAME SORT" (Intro Sec.12). A determinate particular idea,
   SELECTIVELY ATTENDED TO, does duty for a class: the geometer draws
   THIS black inch-long line and proves a theorem about ALL lines.
 6 COROLLARY AGAINST THE WHOLE TRADITION: words do not always stand for
   ideas at all; language also RAISES PASSION, PROMPTS ACTION and
   DISPOSES THE MIND (Intro Sec.19-20). This is a genuine anticipation
   of the later claim that meaning is USE.
 7 METAPHYSICAL PAYLOAD: Locke's MATERIAL SUBSTANCE IN GENERAL and
   SUBSTRATUM are precisely abstract general ideas - the abstract idea
   of BEING plus the relative notion of SUPPORTING. If abstraction is
   impossible, "matter" is not a mysterious unknown but a MEANINGLESS
   EXPRESSION. IMMATERIALISM RESTS ON THE NOMINALISM, NOT THE REVERSE.
        |
        v
WHAT IS DENIED AND WHAT IS NOT
   DENIED: ABSTRACT general ideas.
   NOT DENIED: general ideas, general words, general knowledge. Geometry
   proceeds perfectly well on Berkeley's account.
        |
        v
TWO LIMITS TO STATE YOURSELF
   LIMIT 1 The argument works only against an IMAGISTIC reading of
     ideas. Locke's DETERMINABLE reply - undetermined, not contradictory
     - escapes step 3.
   LIMIT 2 THE UNPAID BILL: what fixes the RESPECT under which the
     particular idea represents its class? If the answer is a further
```
