#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 24/48: Berkeley: arguments 1 and 2 - the semantic argument and the likeness principle

```ascii-master
==============================================================================
ARGUMENT 1 - SEMANTIC / ANALYTIC                    Principles Sec.1-4
 1 The objects of human knowledge are IDEAS: (a) imprinted on the
   senses, (b) perceived by attending to the passions and operations of
   the mind, (c) formed by memory and imagination.
 2 "SENSIBLE THINGS" MEANS things IMMEDIATELY perceived by sense.
   (This is the sense of the phrase quoted in PYQ 2021 Q1(c).)
 3 An apple, a stone, a tree, a book are NOTHING BUT COLLECTIONS of such
   immediately perceived ideas.
 4 It is impossible that ideas should exist unperceived - their ESSE is
   PERCIPI; existence for them CONSISTS IN being perceived.
 5 THEREFORE for sensible things ESSE EST PERCIPI (Sec.3), and to speak
   of their "absolute existence without relation to their being
   perceived" is UNINTELLIGIBLE.
   PRESUPPOSITION: step 3 is a DEFINITION, not a discovery. Grant it and
   immaterialism follows in four lines; deny it and nothing follows.
   THE WORD DOING THE WORK: "IMMEDIATELY". Locke held we perceive ideas
   that REPRESENT objects, so the object is reached MEDIATELY, by
   inference. Berkeley's definition removes the inference, and with it
   the veil and the sceptical problem the veil generated.
   BERKELEY'S OWN ILLUSTRATION (Sec.3): "The table I write on, I say,
   exists; that is, I see and feel it."
        |
ARGUMENT 2 - THE LIKENESS PRINCIPLE                    Principles Sec.8
 1 Locke says our ideas of primary qualities RESEMBLE qualities in
   bodies.
 2 But "AN IDEA CAN BE LIKE NOTHING BUT AN IDEA" - a colour or figure
   can be like nothing but another colour or figure.
 3 If the supposed originals are UNPERCEIVABLE, no comparison with them
   is even possible; a resemblance-claim we could NEVER IN PRINCIPLE
   CHECK is empty.
 4 THEREFORE the representative-realist relation of resemblance between
   idea and material quality is UNINTELLIGIBLE.
   PRESUPPOSITION: resemblance requires COMPARABILITY BY A PERCEIVER.
   STRONGEST OBJECTION: STRUCTURAL or MATHEMATICAL resemblance
   (ISOMORPHISM) does not require qualitative likeness - a map resembles
   terrain without being terrain-coloured. This is the standard modern
   RESCUE OF LOCKE, and it concedes the qualitative point while saving
   the representational relation.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 25/48: Berkeley: arguments 3 and 4 - parity and the Master Argument

```ascii-master
==============================================================================
ARGUMENT 3 - PARITY                     Principles Sec.9-15; Dialogues I
   LOGICAL FORM: a PARITY argument. Every relativity consideration Locke
   accepts for SECONDARY qualities - variation with observer, with
   organ, with medium - applies equally to PRIMARY ones:
      apparent SIZE varies with distance
      MOTION and rest are relative to the frame of reference
      SHAPE varies with the angle of view
      EXTENSION cannot be conceived without colour or some visual
        quality; FIGURE is inseparable from extension
   1 Locke ADMITS secondary qualities are mind-dependent.
   2 Primary qualities are EQUALLY mind-dependent by the same tests.
   3 THEREFORE all qualities are ideas.
   4 THEREFORE there is NOTHING LEFT for "material substance" to be.
   PRESUPPOSITION: perceptual relativity ENTAILS mind-dependence.
   OBJECTION: it does NOT. That a thing looks different from different
   positions is what one should EXPECT of a mind-INDEPENDENT thing with
   a real shape. So parity is suggestive, not decisive - which is why
   the 2024 answer must identify the LIKENESS principle as the weapon
   that actually does the work.
        |
ARGUMENT 4 - THE MASTER ARGUMENT                   Principles Sec.22-23
 1 Berkeley stakes everything on one challenge: CONCEIVE ONE EXTENDED
   MOVABLE BODY EXISTING UNCONCEIVED, and he will concede materialism.
 2 You reply that you can easily conceive a tree standing alone in a
   park with nobody by.
 3 But IN DOING SO YOU ARE CONCEIVING IT - so you have framed ideas in
   your own mind and have NOT conceived the tree UNCONCEIVED at all.
 4 THEREFORE the supposition is SELF-REFUTING; unperceived existence
   cannot even be entertained.
   PRESUPPOSITION (AND THE FATAL ONE): that conceiving "X EXISTING
   UNCONCEIVED" requires the ACT of conceiving to be part of the CONTENT
   conceived.
   STRONGEST OBJECTION (Russell; refined by Prior and Gallois): a SCOPE
   or USE-MENTION confusion. What is inconceivable is "I conceive OF X
   THAT IT IS NOT CONCEIVED BY ME"; what is required is only "I conceive
   THAT X exists and is not conceived" - perfectly coherent, exactly as
   I can THINK ABOUT an unthought-of number.
   BERKELEY'S BEST REPLY: for him there is no OBJECT over and above the
   IDEA, so the distinction the objector needs is precisely what is at
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 27/48: Berkeley: argument 5 - continuity, spirits, notions and the divine language

```ascii-master
==============================================================================
ARGUMENT 5 - GOD AS GUARANTOR   Principles Sec.6, 30-33, 48; Dialogues II
 1 IDEAS ARE PASSIVE and inert; they cannot cause anything.
 2 Yet the ideas of sense come to me INVOLUNTARILY and in a steady,
   regular order.
 3 Whatever produces them must be an ACTIVE SPIRIT, and one whose power
   and wisdom EXCEED MINE.
 4 THEREFORE GOD. The "laws of nature" are the SETTLED GRAMMAR of God's
   LANGUAGE of ideas - regularities are SIGNS, not causes.
 5 THEREFORE objects PERSIST unperceived by finite minds because they
   are always perceived by the INFINITE MIND.
   "Does the tree exist when no one perceives it?" YES - God perceives.
   PRESUPPOSITION: only spirits are active causes (Berkeley's
   VOLUNTARISM).
   OBJECTION: this makes "existence unperceived" back into a HYPOTHESIS
   ABOUT AN UNOBSERVED BEING - the very move Berkeley forbade Locke.
        |
        v
THE FINAL ONTOLOGY - TWO CATEGORIES, NOTHING ELSE
   SPIRITS (ACTIVE substances that perceive and will)
     GOD - infinite; produces the ideas of sense; sustains continuity
     FINITE SPIRITS - my own known by inner awareness; OTHERS by ANALOGY
     from purposive signs and behaviour
   IDEAS (PASSIVE)
     OF SENSE - involuntary, vivid, coherent, LAW-GOVERNED
     OF IMAGINATION - comparatively voluntary, faint, irregular
        |
        v
HOW SPIRIT IS KNOWN: BY NOTION, NOT BY IDEA    Sec.27, 89, 140
   REASON: an idea, being PASSIVE, could never PICTURE an ACTIVITY.
   Notions also cover RELATIONS, which are likewise not pictured.
   CRITICS: the asymmetry is AD HOC - an exemption introduced to protect
     the favoured posit.
   BERKELEY: the asymmetry is FORCED, not chosen; the objection assumes
     that all knowledge is idea-shaped, which is what he denies once
     ACTIVITY enters the ontology.
   HUME'S TEST: he refuses the exemption and dissolves SPIRITUAL
     substance too - which is exactly how much weight it was carrying.
        |
        v
```

```ascii-master
==============================================================================
THE REVERSAL THAT MUST BE STATED OR THE POSITION IS MISDESCRIBED
   Berkeley insists he is NOT denying tables and chairs; he denies only
   the PHILOSOPHER'S FICTION of matter. The ordinary person never
   believed in Locke's veil of perception - they believe they perceive
   things DIRECTLY, AND SO DOES BERKELEY.
   He accuses the MATERIALISTS of undermining common sense by positing
   an unknowable world behind appearances, and of thereby manufacturing
   the sceptical problem they then fail to solve.
   HONEST LIMIT: the defence is strong about OBJECTS and weaker about
   CAUSES. The plain man also believes that fire heats water, whereas
   for Berkeley fire is a SIGN and God is the AGENT.

+---------------------------+---------------------------------------+

|                           | IMAGINATION are voluntary, faint,     |
|                           | irregular                             |
+---------------------------+---------------------------------------+
| Illusion - the bent oar,  | the presented idea GENUINELY EXISTS;  |
| the distant tower?        | error lies in the JUDGMENT about what |
|                           | further ideas will follow             |

|                           | sequences in God's language of ideas  |

|                           | order                                 |
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 30/48: Berkeley: common sense, illusion, science, other minds and anti-scepticism (continued)

```ascii-master
==============================================================================
BERKELEY IS AN ANTI-SCEPTIC
   He claims to REFUTE scepticism: it is the MATERIALIST who produces
   doubt by positing an unknowable world behind ideas. Identify reality
   with ideas immediately perceived and certainty returns; God
   guarantees continuity. His position is DOGMATIC THEISTIC IDEALISM -
   a thesis about what EXISTS, not a doubt about knowledge.
NEVER WRITE: "Berkeley denies the external world" or "Berkeley is a
sceptic". Both are examiner-flagged misreadings.
```

```ascii-master

ALL CONTENTS OF THE MIND = PERCEPTIONS
   IMPRESSIONS (FORCE AND VIVACITY)      IDEAS (FAINT COPIES)
   of SENSATION: colours, sounds,        MEMORY: retains order and

   of REFLECTION: passions, emotions,    IMAGINATION: free to separate
     the felt determination of the mind    and recombine
   CRITERION: force and vivacity - a difference of DEGREE. Hume accepts
   that in fever or madness the difference can blur; this is a working
   psychology, not a definition by essence.

THE COPY PRINCIPLE
   every SIMPLE idea derives from a corresponding SIMPLE impression.
   COMPLEX ideas may be new combinations (a golden mountain).
   If no source impression can be located, the alleged idea is SUSPECT,
   OBSCURE OR FICTITIOUS - not automatically "meaningless" in the later
   logical-positivist sense. That stronger reading belongs to Topic 07.
        |
        v
THE CONCEDED EXCEPTION - THE MISSING SHADE OF BLUE
                                        Treatise I.i.1; Enquiry II
   A man acquainted with every shade of blue but one, shown the spectrum
   with a gap, CAN form the idea of the missing shade from imagination
   alone - a simple idea with no antecedent impression. Hume calls the
   instance "SO SINGULAR" that it is not worth altering his maxim for it.
   WHY IT MATTERS FOR MARKS: it shows the copy principle is an EMPIRICAL
     GENERALISATION, not an analytic truth - so the demolitions of
     substance, self and necessary connexion rest on a premise its own
     author admits admits an exception. Deploying it converts a
     DESCRIPTIVE answer into a CRITICAL one.
   HUME'S AVAILABLE REPLY: the shade is generated by INTERPOLATION
     BETWEEN impressions actually had, so the principle's SPIRIT
     survives even if its LETTER does not.
        |
        v
THE THREE PRINCIPLES OF ASSOCIATION - the mind's gentle force
   RESEMBLANCE | CONTIGUITY in time or place | CAUSE AND EFFECT
   These are PSYCHOLOGICAL mechanisms, NOT logical relations - and they
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 35/48: Hume: the fork, the dissolution of substance and the belief in body

```ascii-master
==============================================================================
HUME'S FORK
+-------------------------------+-------------------------------+
| RELATIONS OF IDEAS            | MATTERS OF FACT               |
| known A PRIORI, by thought    | known A POSTERIORI, require   |
| alone                         | experience                    |
| NECESSARY and certain; denial | CONTINGENT; the contrary is   |
| is SELF-CONTRADICTORY         | ALWAYS CONCEIVABLE            |
| "2+2=4"; "a bachelor is       | "the sun will rise tomorrow"; |
| unmarried"                    | "fire causes heat"            |
| do NOT depend on what exists  | DEPEND on how the world       |
| in the world                  | actually is                   |
+-------------------------------+-------------------------------+
   CONSEQUENCE: anything that is NEITHER is meaningless - "COMMIT IT
   THEN TO THE FLAMES" (Enquiry XII), aimed at "divinity or school
   metaphysics".
   ANCESTRY: the direct ancestor of the logical positivists'
   verification principle - worth ONE CLAUSE, not a paragraph.
   2018 Q1(a) IN ONE LINE: "2+2=4" is a relation of ideas whose denial
   is contradictory; "the Sun will rise tomorrow" is a matter of fact
   whose denial remains perfectly CONCEIVABLE. NOT the same necessity,
   and NOT merely a difference of probability.
   REFLEXIVE OBJECTION: is the FORK itself a relation of ideas or a
   matter of fact? If neither, it burns itself. HUME'S AVAILABLE ANSWER:
   it is a clarification of the MEANINGS OF TERMS - a meta-level
   classification, not a first-order claim.
        |
        v
FIRST CASUALTY - SUBSTANCE
   Do we have an IMPRESSION of substance? NO. We have impressions of
   particular qualities - redness, hardness, sweetness - never of an
   underlying substrate. THEREFORE "substance" is a FICTION OF THE
   IMAGINATION: the mind's habit of grouping constantly conjoined
   qualities and INVENTING a support for them.
   SCOPE: MATERIAL and SPIRITUAL substance alike. THIS IS WHERE HUME
   PASSES BERKELEY, who retained spirit.
   RESULT: an OBJECT is a stable BUNDLE; the SELF is a flowing bundle.
        |
        v
THE BELIEF IN BODY                                 Treatise I.iv.2
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 36/48: Hume: the fork, the dissolution of substance and the belief in body (continued)

```ascii-master
==============================================================================
   Sense presents only INTERRUPTED perceptions; it does not present an
   object existing CONTINUOUSLY while unperceived or DISTINCTLY beyond
   perception.
   CONSTANCY of similar perceptions + COHERENCE in their reappearance
   -> the IMAGINATION FEIGNS continued objects and smooths the gaps.
   The philosophers' DOUBLE-EXISTENCE theory - private perceptions
   representing independent objects - CANNOT BE VERIFIED, because all
   comparison remains WITHIN perceptions.
   CRUCIAL QUALIFICATION: Hume does NOT demonstrate that no external
   world exists. Sceptical reflection undermines the RATIONAL
   JUSTIFICATION; NATURE restores the unavoidable belief in bodies.
   THE PATTERN OF HUMEAN NATURALISM: explain why a belief is
   psychologically inevitable AFTER showing that reason cannot establish
   its metaphysical object.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 37/48: Hume: the bundle self, the Appendix retraction and Kant's reply

```ascii-master
==============================================================================
THE ARGUMENT                                        Treatise I.iv.6
 1 Apply the COPY PRINCIPLE to the self: from which impression is the
   idea of a self copied? It would have to be CONSTANT AND INVARIABLE
   through the whole course of a life.
 2 INTROSPECTION delivers only particular perceptions - heat, cold,
   light, shade, love, hatred, pain, pleasure - in PERPETUAL FLUX.
 3 NO constant impression is found.
 4 THEREFORE the self is "NOTHING BUT A BUNDLE OR COLLECTION OF
   DIFFERENT PERCEPTIONS, WHICH SUCCEED EACH OTHER WITH AN INCONCEIVABLE
   RAPIDITY, AND ARE IN A PERPETUAL FLUX AND MOVEMENT."
   WHAT IS DENIED: a SIMPLE, IDENTICAL SELF-SUBSTANCE.
   WHAT IS NOT DENIED: the perceptions, which are REAL.
        |
        v
WHY THE ILLUSION OF UNITY?
   RESEMBLANCE - present ideas resemble past memories, creating a felt
     continuity
   CAUSATION - one perception seems to produce the next; memory links
     them into a chain
   -> the IMAGINATION passes SMOOTHLY along the series and MISTAKES AN
      EASY TRANSITION FOR AN IDENTICAL OBJECT: the FICTION of identity
        |
        v
THE APPENDIX RETRACTION - THE PAGE THAT EARNS MARKS
   Hume cannot explain what BINDS the bundle: "ALL MY HOPES VANISH WHEN
   I COME TO EXPLAIN THE PRINCIPLES THAT UNITE OUR SUCCESSIVE
   PERCEPTIONS."
   DIAGNOSIS: he has denied BOTH a substance AND any real connexion
     between distinct existences, so he has removed in advance every
     resource a principle of unity could have used.
   THEREFORE the honest verdict: THE FAILURE INDICTS THE ATOMISM OF
     IMPRESSIONS, NOT THE SELF.
   TEXTUAL NOTE: the Enquiry SILENTLY DROPS personal identity.
        |
```

```ascii-master
==============================================================================
FRONT 1 - THE CAUSAL / COSMOLOGICAL ARGUMENT
   We have NO IMPRESSION of a "first cause" or of causation as NECESSARY
   CONNEXION. We therefore cannot legitimately infer from experienced
   effects to an UNEXPERIENCED TRANSCENDENT CAUSE.
   CONSEQUENCE: the argument LOCKE used is cut off AT ITS PREMISE - the
   cleanest single link between Locke's God and Hume's scepticism.
        |
FRONT 2 - THE DESIGN / TELEOLOGICAL ARGUMENT
                              Dialogues concerning Natural Religion
   FOUR SEPARATE OBJECTIONS - give all four, not "weak analogy" alone:
   1 THE ANALOGY IS WEAK: universe : designer :: watch : watchmaker.
     The universe is not sufficiently similar to a MANUFACTURED ARTIFACT.
   2 EVEN IF GRANTED it proves at most a FINITE ARCHITECT working on
     GIVEN MATTER - not an omnipotent, omniscient, morally perfect
     creator.
   3 ORDER MIGHT ARISE from natural principles - matter's inherent
     organising tendencies - WITHOUT invoking intelligence.
   4 THE PROBLEM OF EVIL undermines any inference from the world to a
     PERFECTLY GOOD designer.
        |
FRONT 3 - MIRACLES                                       Enquiry X
   A miracle violates the laws of nature. Testimony in favour of a
   miracle is ALWAYS LESS PROBABLE than the testimony being false.
   THEREFORE it is never rational to believe miracle-reports on
   TESTIMONY ALONE.
   OWNERSHIP NOTE: the miracle argument is SECONDARY ENRICHMENT here;
   Hume's critique of the PROOFS is CORE, because "God" is printed in
   the syllabus clause.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 41/48: Hume on causation - the negative argument and the two definitions

```ascii-master
==============================================================================
(A) THE NEGATIVE ARGUMENT ON NECESSARY CONNEXION
                                Treatise I.iii.14; Enquiry VII
 1 COPY PRINCIPLE: every simple idea derives from a simple impression.
 2 So if we have a genuine idea of NECESSARY CONNEXION, locate its
   SOURCE IMPRESSION.
 3 EXTERNAL SEARCH: in any SINGLE instance of causation - one billiard
   ball striking another - sense delivers only PRIORITY, CONTIGUITY and
   the SUCCESSION of qualities. No impression of a POWER or TIE.
 4 REPETITION ADDS NO NEW QUALITY TO THE OBJECTS: the hundredth
   collision is qualitatively no different from the first.
 5 INTERNAL SEARCH: volition does no better. I have NO IMPRESSION of HOW
   the will moves the limb; the actual mechanism - nerve, muscle - is
   unknown to consciousness. This rules out the CARTESIAN and the
   OCCASIONALIST appeal alike. (SCRIPTS OMIT THIS STEP; IT IS WHAT MAKES
   THE CONCLUSION GENERAL RATHER THAN ANECDOTAL.)
 6 But repetition DOES produce a change in THE MIND: after constant
   conjunction the mind is DETERMINED to pass from the idea of A to the
   idea of B. That felt determination is an IMPRESSION OF REFLECTION.
 7 THEREFORE the idea of necessary connexion is COPIED FROM THAT
   INTERNAL IMPRESSION. Necessity "IS SOMETHING THAT EXISTS IN THE MIND,
   NOT IN OBJECTS."
 8 THEREFORE TWO DEFINITIONS OF CAUSE FOLLOW:
+---------------------------------+-------------------------------+
| (i) OBJECTIVE / PHILOSOPHICAL   | (ii) SUBJECTIVE / NATURAL     |
| an object followed by another,  | an object followed by another,|
| where ALL OBJECTS SIMILAR to    | whose appearance CONVEYS THE  |
| the first are followed by       | THOUGHT to the other          |
| objects similar to the second   |                               |
| regularity-based, EXTERNAL      | psychological, INTERNAL       |
+---------------------------------+-------------------------------+
   NAMING BOTH IS THE SINGLE HIGHEST-VALUE MOVE IN ANY HUME-ON-CAUSATION
   ANSWER. TENSION TO FLAG: the two pull apart and Hume NEVER RECONCILES
   them - the origin of the modern REGULARITY vs PROJECTIVIST debate.
   MECHANISM: CUSTOM OR HABIT, "the great guide of human life" (Enq. V).
PRESUPPOSITIONS
   P1 the copy principle is exceptionless
   P2 single and repeated instances can be compared for ADDED content -
      i.e. causation must be visible in ONE instance if objective at all
   P3 impressions are ATOMIC and SEPARABLE, so no relation is given WITH
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 43/48: Hume: the induction dilemma, Kant's Second Analogy and mitigated scepticism

```ascii-master
==============================================================================
(B) THE PROBLEM OF INDUCTION AS A FORMAL DILEMMA          Enquiry IV
 1 All reasoning about matters of fact beyond present sense and memory
   rests on the relation of CAUSE AND EFFECT.
 2 Causal inference rests on the UNIFORMITY PRINCIPLE (UP): the future
   will resemble the past; unobserved cases resemble observed ones.
 3 UP can be established ONLY DEMONSTRATIVELY OR PROBABLY.
   <== THE VULNERABLE STEP: this is the FORK APPLIED REFLEXIVELY
 4 NOT DEMONSTRATIVELY: "the course of nature may change" implies no
   contradiction - it is perfectly CONCEIVABLE.
 5 NOT PROBABLY: every probable or experiential argument ALREADY
   PRESUPPOSES UP, so the argument would be CIRCULAR.
 6 THEREFORE UP has NO RATIONAL FOUNDATION.
 7 THEREFORE inductive inference is produced not by REASON but by
   CUSTOM OR HABIT.
   EVERY SERIOUS REPLY ATTACKS STEP 3:
     KANT     supplies a THIRD category - the SYNTHETIC A PRIORI
     POPPER   denies that science needs induction at all
     STRAWSON argues that demanding a justification of induction AS A
              WHOLE misapplies the very standards induction constitutes
   EXECUTABLE VERDICT: the sceptical conclusion depends on the
   EXHAUSTIVENESS of a dichotomy that IS the fork - so Hume's scepticism
   about induction is the SHADOW CAST BY HIS CLASSIFICATION OF
   PROPOSITIONS, and survives only as long as the fork does.
        |
        v
(C) KANT'S RESPONSE - THE ROUTED REPLY IN 2025 Q2(c)
   - Kant AGREES that necessary connection is not copied from a sensory
     impression. HE ACCEPTS THE NEGATIVE RESULT.
   - SECOND ANALOGY: the category of CAUSE is an A PRIORI RULE required
     to distinguish OBJECTIVE event-succession from the merely
     SUBJECTIVE succession of my perceptions. Without a rule, the
     difference between a SHIP DRIFTING DOWNSTREAM and MY EYE WANDERING
     OVER A HOUSE cannot be drawn.
   - "Every event has a cause" is therefore SYNTHETIC A PRIORI FOR
     POSSIBLE EXPERIENCE: the understanding supplies necessary temporal
     ordering to phenomena.
   - LIMIT: this rescues objective empirical causation WITHOUT claiming
     knowledge of causality among THINGS IN THEMSELVES.
   - HUMEAN CHALLENGE: has Kant PROVED the category, or BUILT IT INTO
```

```ascii-master
     the definition of objective experience? KANT'S REPLY is

(D) MITIGATED SCEPTICISM - THE POSITIVE DOCTRINE       Enquiry XII
   EXCESSIVE / PYRRHONIAN: total suspension of judgment - UNLIVEABLE
   MITIGATED: limit enquiry to what experience can answer; avoid
     speculation beyond possible experience (substance, God, the soul);
     PROPORTION BELIEF TO EVIDENCE; rely on the natural beliefs custom
     compels
   STANDING OBJECTION: Hume USES induction while denying its rational
     basis. REPLY: nature forces belief; philosophy reveals its
     groundlessness; both can be true without practical contradiction.
     RESIDUAL: stable ONLY IF natural beliefs are exempt from the demand
     for justification - which concedes that not all belief needs reason.
```

```ascii-master
   EXAMINE = state AND test; rule on the objection's force
   CRITICALLY ANALYSE = exposition PLUS a delivered verdict

   "IS A INSTRUMENTAL IN B?" = a CAUSAL-EXPLANATORY ruling
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 48/48: PYQ routes, directive decoder, answer spines, provenance, traps and conclusion (continued)

```ascii-master
==============================================================================
   15-mark: frame -> Body A (the empiricist argument, complete) ->
     Body B (the routed reply) -> assessment -> close
   20-mark: frame -> full analysis -> positive account -> three
     objections -> ruling that names the load-bearing premise
PROVENANCE RULES THAT PROTECT MARKS
   "tabula rasa" is a LATER LABEL; Locke writes "white paper, void of
     all characters" (II.i.2)
   "esse est percipi" is Berkeley's own Latin (Sec.3); "aut percipere"
     is a COMMENTATORS' EXPANSION
   Essay II.xxvii was ADDED IN 1694; the Essay appeared in 1689 with
     1690 on the title page
   Treatise =/= Enquiry: the Enquiry DROPS personal identity; published
     1748 as Philosophical Essays, RENAMED 1758
   "Reason is the slave of the passions" is Treatise II.iii.3 - ETHICS,
     not epistemology
   Samuel Johnson's stone is RECEPTION, not argument
TRAP SHEET - TEN LINES
   1 "Berkeley denies the external world" -> he denies MATTER AS AN
     UNPERCEIVING SUBSTRATUM
   2 "Hume denies causation" -> he denies OBJECTIVE NECESSARY CONNEXION
   3 "Secondary qualities are illusions" -> they are POWERS
   4 "Locke denies substance" -> he denies KNOWLEDGE OF ITS NATURE
   5 "tabula rasa" quoted as Locke's words -> it is the tradition's
   6 "Hume says the self does not exist" -> no SIMPLE IDENTICAL
     SUBSTANCE; the perceptions are real
   7 "Kant proves the soul" -> he supplies a FORMAL CONDITION
   8 Berkeley called a sceptic -> he is an ANTI-SCEPTIC
   9 Berkeley called an early Hegel -> different in KIND
  10 Quine's "Two Dogmas of Empiricism" imported here -> Topic 11
QUALIFIED CONCLUSION
   Empiricism consistently applied ends in a scepticism nobody can live.
   That outcome DIAGNOSES the founding rule as INCOMPLETE rather than
   refuting philosophy - which is exactly the conclusion Kant draws when
   he keeps Hume's negative results and attacks the ATOMISM OF
   IMPRESSIONS that produced them. The slide from Locke to Hume is
   therefore RIGOUR, not decadence, and the next syllabus item begins
   where this one ends.
```
